# Projects

Longer write-ups on selected work. Everything here links back to the public repository (and live site, where there is one) so the code can speak for itself.

## At a glance

| Project | Stack | What it is for | Start here |
|---|---|---|---|
| CompileLab | Python standard library; browser JavaScript engine; exact finite Boolean knowledge compilation, ROBDD order/canonicity and existential-projection evidence | Exact finite Boolean knowledge compilation: the same function needs 6 to 9 nonterminals across all 24 variable orders, and projecting `x0 OR x1` keeps two assignments from three full models — a projected model is not a sum of hidden completions | [Case study](compilelab.md) · [Live workbench](https://midya8.github.io/compilelab/) |
| FireLab | Python standard library; browser JavaScript engine; exact finite Petri-net firing-count, ordering and goal-reachability evidence | Exact finite resource planning: counts (1,0,0) balance exactly for t0 yet t0 cannot fire with no A token — balance is not an execution | [Case study](firelab.md) · [Live inspector](https://midya8.github.io/firelab/) |
| OrderLab | Python standard library; independent JavaScript inspector; exact finite Kemeny rank aggregation, subset-DP optima and local-descent evidence | Exact finite rank aggregation: a strict adjacent-local optimum can still lose globally — start at cost 10, every adjacent swap worse, yet the unique global optimum costs 8, and the independent-pair bound of 7 is not a transitive order | [Case study](orderlab.md) · [Live inspector](https://midya8.github.io/orderlab/) |
| RectLab | Python standard library; browser JavaScript inspector; exact finite Boolean factorization, minimum covers/partitions and fooling-set evidence | Exact finite Boolean factorization: the default 4×4 matrix has 68 unrestricted minimum covers but only 13 maximal-only ones, and a largest fooling set of 3 does not certify a minimum cover of 4 — a cover is not a partition | [Case study](rectlab.md) · [Live inspector](https://midya8.github.io/rectlab/) |
| RiskLab | Python standard library; browser JavaScript inspector; exact finite scenario-tree decisions under static and nested CVaR | Exact finite scenario-tree decisions: the unique static optimum p3 (static CVaR 7/3) is not the nested optimum p2 (nested CVaR 22/9), and p3's reached continuation would change — a root optimum is not a continuation optimum | [Case study](risklab.md) · [Live inspector](https://midya8.github.io/risklab/) |
| ParetoLab | Python standard library; browser JavaScript inspector; exact finite biobjective DAG planning, scalarization intervals and Pareto-label pruning evidence | Exact finite biobjective planning: three paths (0,12), (7,7) and (12,0) are all nondominated, yet no positive weighted sum selects the middle one — nondominated is not supported | [Case study](paretolab.md) · [Live inspector](https://midya8.github.io/paretolab/) |
| MatchLab | Python standard library; browser JavaScript inspector; exact finite two-sided matching, deferred-acceptance traces and stable-lattice evidence | Exact finite two-sided matching: the cheapest assignment costs 3 but is blocked, while the cheapest stable assignment costs 13 — stable is not cost-optimal | [Case study](matchlab.md) · [Live inspector](https://midya8.github.io/matchlab/) |
| AuctionLab | Python standard library; browser JavaScript inspector; exact finite VCG allocation, bundle-menu and coalition-deficit evidence | Exact finite VCG allocation evidence: two items and three bidders reach welfare 12 with revenue 8, yet the seller-inclusive coalition is short by 2 — truthful alone is not protected together | [Case study](auctionlab.md) · [Live inspector](https://midya8.github.io/auctionlab/) |
| CredalLab | Python standard library; independent BigInt JavaScript inspector; exact finite probabilistic satisfiability and shared-belief decision evidence | Exact finite probability knowledge bases: three events can each demand probability 1 while no joint distribution satisfies them — and one action can beat every rival at a different belief yet have no single belief where it wins, excluded by a 50/50 mixture by 1/2 everywhere | [Case study](credallab.md) · [Live inspector](https://midya8.github.io/credallab/) · [Live decision inspector](https://midya8.github.io/credallab/decisions.html) |
| ChannelLab | Python standard library; independent BigInt JavaScript inspector; exact finite Blackwell comparison and all-prior decision atlas | Exact finite binary experiment comparison: two channels can tie at 3/4 classification accuracy while neither simulates the other — every garbling kernel, decision witness and prior-knot policy range shown | [Case study](channellab.md) · [Live inspector](https://midya8.github.io/channellab/) |
| StableLab | Python standard library; independent JavaScript workbench; exact finite stable-model, strong-equivalence and uniform-equivalence evidence | Finite answer-set evidence for normal logic programs: a supported, minimal model can still fail to be stable — every candidate, reduct and derivation round shown, with complete strong- and uniform-equivalence atlases that separate matching answers from safe replacement | [Case study](stablelab.md) · [Live workbench](https://midya8.github.io/stablelab/) |
| EpistemLab | Python standard library; independent JavaScript workbench; exact finite S5 models, public-announcement updates and Boolean disclosure atlases | Exact finite public-announcement evidence from supplied S5 models: every world removed, every surviving information block and every subformula truth set shown — a truthful announcement can make its own content false, and a restriction is not an announcement | [Case study](epistemlab.md) · [Live workbench](https://midya8.github.io/epistemlab/) |
| CPNetLab | Python standard library; independent JavaScript workbench; exact finite CP-net enumeration, improving-flip paths, compatible-ranking counts and whole-row subset atlases | Exact finite qualitative preferences from supplied tables: every outcome and improving flip enumerated, shortest paths or closed no-path cuts, every compatible strict ranking counted — a ranking is not an entailed preference, and minimal is not minimum among whole-row withdrawals | [Case study](cpnetlab.md) · [Live workbench](https://midya8.github.io/cpnetlab/) |
| RewriteLab | Python standard library; independent JavaScript engines; exact rewrite-graph, budget-barrier, cost-atlas and continuous-region analysis | Exact arithmetic-optimization lab where a cheap equivalent expression can fit the grammar yet stay unreachable — x*(1+1) → x+x first fits at budget 5 and is first reachable at 7 — with budget barriers, a 512-triple cost atlas and complete continuous optimum regions, every proof and candidate checked by independent enumeration | [Case study](rewritelab.md) · [Live inspector](https://midya8.github.io/rewritelab/) |
| HingeLab | Python standard library; independent JavaScript engines; exact rational ReLU regions, all-reference atlas and all-radius robustness profile | Exact robust references and interval lengths for every radius: a closed ball's robustness depends on which class owns the boundary, and zero length is not absence — every radius, reference and existence interval computed exactly and checked by independent finite exhaustion | [Case study](hingelab.md) · [Live inspector](https://midya8.github.io/hingelab/) |
| WarpLab | Python standard library; independent JavaScript engines; soft-DTW paths, length atlas and inverse response | Inspect Gibbs cell/edge laws and cost Hessian products, separate path lengths, then test whether a bounded penalty can reach a target expected length | [Case study](warplab.md) · [Live inspector](https://midya8.github.io/warplab/) |
| SynthLab | Python standard library; independent JavaScript engines; exact CEGIS synthesis, finite teaching-set atlas and adaptive query policies | Counterexample-guided bit-vector program synthesis where fitting the examples is not identifying the function — every iteration, eliminated candidate and truth table replayable, with exact teaching sets and adaptive query policies that let the answer choose the next question | [Case study](synthlab.md) · [Live inspector](https://midya8.github.io/synthlab/) |
| DFALab | Python standard library; independent JavaScript engines; exact L* learning, W-method conformance and fault-atlas subset selection | Tiny active-learning inspector where a closed observation table can still propose the wrong language — every membership query, equivalence product and distinguishing experiment is replayable, with exhaustive three-state teacher coverage and exact W-suite subset minima | [Case study](dfalab.md) · [Live inspector](https://midya8.github.io/dfalab/) |
| EUFLab | Python standard library; independent JavaScript relation engine; exact congruence-closure proof DAGs, finite countermodels and costed repair plans | Proof-producing ground-equality inspector with five families — proof DAGs, finite-model atlas, domain-cap debugger, costed repair planner and exact repair-cost sensitivity — where deleting an assertion is not negating it, and a minimal repair need not have minimum size | [Case study](euflab.md) · [Live inspector](https://midya8.github.io/euflab/) |
| STNLab | Python standard library; independent JavaScript solver; exact Floyd-Warshall/Bellman-Ford bounds with attaining schedules, rays and minimal conflicts/repairs | Tiny temporal-network inspector where every implied time difference carries a complete schedule attaining the bound or an explicit ray proving it unbounded — a tight bound is not an attained bound, and minimal is not minimum among conflict repairs | [Case study](stnlab.md) · [Live inspector](https://midya8.github.io/stnlab/) |
| HallLab | Python standard library; independent JavaScript engine; exact matching/counting and Hall-subset witnesses; 36 Python tests | Tiny constraint-based AI assignment with every supported variable/value backed by a complete injective assignment and every rejected edge explained by a forced-residual Hall obstruction — local support is not a shared solution, and minimal is not minimum among scope-removal repairs; v0.4 adds an alternating-path inspector, where a spare value is not a cycle — three singleton alternating SCCs, both domain edges supported, 5,058 domain graphs | [Case study](halllab.md) · [Live inspector](https://midya8.github.io/halllab/) · [Live alternating inspector](https://midya8.github.io/halllab/alternating.html) |
| BuchiLab | Python standard library; independent JavaScript sets; exact nested fixed points, progress ranks and lasso witnesses | Tiny adversarial repeated-goal games with every fixed-point obligation, progress rank and reachable cyclic non-goal SCC visible — a winning state is not a winning policy, and a reachable bad component becomes an exact action-labelled lasso | [Case study](buchilab.md) · [Live inspector](https://midya8.github.io/buchilab/) |
| AbsorbLab | Python fractions; JavaScript BigInt; replayable certificates | Supplied finite Markov chains: first-hit outcomes, strong lumping and exact one-row probability response, with separately solved endpoints and success-conditioned traffic | [Case study](absorblab.md) · [First hit](https://midya8.github.io/absorblab/) · [Lumping](https://midya8.github.io/absorblab/lumping.html) · [Response](https://midya8.github.io/absorblab/response.html) |
| BranchLab | Python standard library; independent JavaScript workbench; exact finite CTL truth sets, fixed-point rounds and subset-image atlas | Explicit finite CTL inspector for supplied models: every subformula truth set, least/greatest fixed-point round and branching certificate is visible — always possible is not inevitable, and a single trace proves only what its quantifier allows; v0.2 adds state-justice fair CTL, where a fair path must meet every justice set | [Case study](branchlab.md) · [Live workbench](https://midya8.github.io/branchlab/) · [Live fairness inspector](https://midya8.github.io/branchlab/fair.html) |
| ProvLab | Python standard library; independent JavaScript workbench; exact finite relational provenance, bag multiplicities and possible-world probabilities | Finite relational-provenance workbench for symbolic data reasoning: shared query variables perform real joins, and reusing a token is one event, not a fresh independent draw — the default p² + pq has bag multiplicity 10 and exact presence probability 1/2, not the ordinary substitution 3/8; v0.2 adds safe conjunctive-query containment, where the same answers can carry different counts; v0.3 adds constraint-aware containment under full rules and keys, where the assumptions change the answer | [Case study](provlab.md) · [Live workbench](https://midya8.github.io/provlab/) · [Live containment lab](https://midya8.github.io/provlab/containment.html) · [Live chase workbench](https://midya8.github.io/provlab/chase.html) |
| ShieldLab | Python standard library; explicit-set JavaScript; 39 Python tests | Tiny safety-game inspector for hand-written agent protocols: every fixed-point obligation, winning move, losing rank and branching proof graph is visible — a one-step-safe action need not preserve safety forever; v0.2 adds a first-goal inspector, where a winning successor can still stall; v0.3 adds a safe-recurrence inspector, where a target visited once need not be visited again — 74,088 three-state configurations | [Case study](shieldlab.md) · [Live inspector](https://midya8.github.io/shieldlab/) · [Live first-goal inspector](https://midya8.github.io/shieldlab/reachavoid.html) · [Live recurrence inspector](https://midya8.github.io/shieldlab/recurrence.html) |
| BisimLab | Python standard library; independent JavaScript sets; exact strong-bisimulation, trace-product, silent-action and explicit-divergence witnesses | Finite agent-protocol equivalence with trace equality checked separately from branching equivalence: every strong-bisimulation refinement, failed step match, Hennessy-Milner witness DAG, a complete trace-product replay with shortest distinguishing paths, and a silent-action inspector where strong, branching and weak bisimilarity give three different answers; v0.4 adds an explicit-divergence inspector, where two states can both loop forever yet only one can diverge inside the class where the visible action stays available | [Case study](bisimlab.md) · [Live inspector](https://midya8.github.io/bisimlab/) · [Live divergence inspector](https://midya8.github.io/bisimlab/divergence.html) |

## AI systems

- **PPOlab** - From-scratch NumPy PPO with a corrected internal-timeout collector, separate bootstrap/trace masks, full GAE credit matrices and sign-dependent clip-gradient audits. Independent scalar JavaScript evaluates all 4,675 checkpoint parameters and real CartPole physics. Sixty-four Python tests retain all 50 earlier tests; seven Node/actual-Chrome tests retain all four earlier tests, which compare 26 full reports/12,408 numbers and all 14,871 original one-step observations. A new exact-rational extension shows the clipped surrogate is not a policy box: on the supplied control the best clipped score is 9/25 while an actual hard ratio box gives 1/5, and every unconstrained optimizer has q0 in [7/25,1] with exact total variation from 9/50 to 9/10. New free-running paths can diverge: the reference run differs on 377 actions despite matching all 30 return lengths. Original checkpoint/results retained; no training-improvement, speed, trajectory-equivalence, KL-bound or universal-solve claim.
  - Case study: [ppolab.md](ppolab.md)
  - Repo: [midya8/ppolab](https://github.com/midya8/ppolab)
  - Live: [Boundary and policy inspector](https://midya8.github.io/ppolab/)

Local-first tools for evaluating, tracing, reviewing and planning LLM and agent workflows, plus the training, adaptation and alignment primitives underneath them — gradient boosting, Gaussian-process regression and expected improvement, entropic optimal transport, exact affine prefix scans and coupled dense-state derivative audits, real-digit training with exact robustness audits and baseline-relative attribution, exact joint-play obedience and private-signal audits, exact zero-sum game audits with shared-table interval certificates, sequential betting evidence with certified confidence-sequence inversion, exact finite-channel privacy audits, safe screening and exact regularization paths, influence-function deletion diagnostics, natural-gradient geometry, variational autoencoding, vector search, decoding strategies, quantization, low-rank adaptation, speculative decoding, embedding distribution tests, exact Shapley attribution, a transformer built from scratch, and inspectable tree learners with exact response partitions, budgeted whole-ensemble search and constrained counterfactual geometry, learned masked-digit reconstruction with strong observed-pixel controls, and real-data unsupervised clustering with full restart and matching/uniqueness audits, and nonnegative parts learning with exact fixed-basis objective certificates, and manual Gaussian-mixture density estimation with constrained-covariance EM, precision-space KKT audits and fixed-model input-gradient geometry, and manual soft-margin SVM learning with replayable pair updates, exact rational weak-duality bounds and editable 30-feature scores, and observed-only rating factorization with manual alternating ridge solves, exact rational references and finite conditional-response audits, and auditable classification forests with exact integer split ordering, complete bootstrap-count witnesses and out-of-bag scores that use only zero-draw trees, and normalized spectral clustering with manual affinity/Laplacian/k-means code, explicitly disclosed NumPy eigensolves, full degree-response derivatives and a basis-invariant projector tangent, and manual linear-chain conditional random fields with analytic likelihood gradients, exact normalization witnesses, hypothetical label-event conditioning, auditable joint posterior path sampling and nonlocal occurrence-count events.

- **TagLab** - Manual linear-chain conditional random fields with analytic likelihood gradients, log-space messages and all 327 optimization states replayed, built from scratch — it makes the normalizer part of the model instead of letting a library hide what the partition function does: a 39-parameter sequence score combines fixed observed features, FROM/TO label transitions and separate start/end scores; manual log-space forward/backward produces the global partition, node and adjacent-label marginals, and expected-minus-observed feature counts give the supervised conditional-likelihood gradient, with Viterbi and token-marginal decisions kept separate. The independent-label control uses exactly the same eight observed features including left/right context, its 15 sequence scores fixed at zero and its remaining 24 weights fitted, so it is a fitted competitor rather than a weak untrained baseline; both families fit three positive L2 penalties and select by validation conditional NLL per token. The losing metric stays visible: the selected chain's test NLL/token is 0.557614 against the independent control's 0.813715, but its Viterbi token errors are worse, 66/164 against 56/164, while marginal token errors tie at 56/164 and whole-sequence errors tie at 15/16 — a better distribution score and a worse best path are different objectives, and the family comparison changes fitted parameters and penalty geometry too, so it is descriptive rather than an isolated causal effect. The fixture is 24 training, 12 validation and 16 test sequences of noisy numeric observations from persistent synthetic states, with seeds, complete examples and labels retained. Every one of 327 stored states retains all parameters, all 39 analytic gradient entries, objective, gradient norm, accepted Armijo step and rejected larger steps; the browser independently recomputes objective and gradient from the original training examples. Three fits stop at the 1e-5 gradient tolerance and three exhaust their 80-update budget; the selected chain exhausts its budget with gradient norm about 0.000629, and convexity plus a decreasing curve do not certify that finite iterate as an optimum. An exact normalization witness keeps the global-versus-local distinction concrete: three allowed paths A/C, B/A and B/C have raw weights [1/9,1,1/9], one global normalizer gives [1/11,9/11,1/11] while equal initial branch scores and local outgoing normalizers give [1/2,9/20,1/20], with five evidence weights computed in exact fractions. V0.2 adds hypothetical label-event conditioning: an allowed label subset at each position, fitted scores fixed, event mass plus conditional node/pair/path witnesses, all 39 log-event derivatives, an empty allowed set exactly impossible with no invented conditional distribution, and a possible rare event whose finite log probability underflows to zero in floating point, distinguished from zero support — 3,336 event witnesses and 4,647 browser states overall, with 31 Python and 12 Node/real-Chrome tests at v0.2. V0.3 adds auditable backward-conditional joint path sampling with a paired independent-node control using the same supplied uniforms; exact pair probabilities and total variation stay separate from descriptive frequencies of 64 seeded draws, each reverse step retaining its probabilities, log probabilities, uniform, CDF, chosen bin and numerical flags. V0.4 adds nonlocal occurrence-count events: the count inspector conditions on exactly r occurrences of a chosen label, and an augmented (count, label) forward/suffix recursion exposes the full original count distribution and reconstructs conditional node/pair probabilities, entropy, a complete best path and all 39 event derivatives — counts are positions, not runs or segments. An independent fair two-position control conditioned on exactly one A permits only AB and BA, each with mass 1/2; node marginals remain halves, but independent draws from them leave the event half the time, and the inspector separately computes the independent-node Poisson-binomial count law — conditioning can create dependence without transition scores, and a correct expected count does not guarantee a valid complete path. 43 Python and 20 Node/Chrome tests cover 600 events per enumerator across sixty fixtures in independent Fraction and BigInt path enumerators, all 39 finite-difference event derivatives, 864 cross-language numerical witnesses and 864 browser queries; the inspectors total 5,895 UI states/queries, and the original v0.1 suite's 22 Python and seven Node tests cover 60 rational path-potential controls, all 39 finite differences, 12,753 scalar gradient checks and 32 complete selected-model sequence witnesses, with real Chrome exercising 327 optimizer states plus 984 model/sequence/focus states. Eleven modules and nine commands reproduce outside the checkout from the installed wheel, including six fresh fits, 327 audits and four exact archive/loader exports, with a lossless three-part compressed archive whose SHA-256 is checked before parsing. This models p(labels|observations), not p(observations); both neighboring observations are available, so it is not streaming inference, and model posteriors are not calibrated real-world confidence — original synthetic sequences, not an NLP benchmark, and the portable xorshift32 mid-bin stream is deterministic, not cryptographic or certified independent entropy; the quadratic count-state computation has a 64-position limit, and near-ties may yield different Python/JavaScript path representatives. [Source](https://github.com/midya8/taglab). [Live sequence and gradient inspector](https://midya8.github.io/taglab/). [Label-event inspector](https://midya8.github.io/taglab/evidence.html). [Count inspector](https://midya8.github.io/taglab/counts.html). [Method and limits](https://github.com/midya8/taglab/blob/main/METHOD.md).
- **SpectralLab** - Normalized spectral clustering with manual affinity/Laplacian/k-means code and explicitly disclosed NumPy eigensolves, built from scratch — it makes the graph the visible choice instead of letting a scatter plot hide which points are neighbors and what their connection means: the affinity and normalized Laplacian are built explicitly, the eigenspace is retained, every row is normalized and manual k-means runs on the embedding, with NumPy supplying only the symmetric eigenvalue solve through LAPACK. The browser reconstructs all graph weights and every selected point's degree, neighbors, cut contributions and three eigenpair equations, with original coordinates and row-normalized embedding side by side; any actual archived graph or point can be selected, colors or a display-only edge threshold changed, and the full point witness exported as JSON — a threshold that hides an edge never changes the fit. Two original seeded 96-point fixtures use noisy interleaving arcs and concentric rings; nine fixed graph rules give 18 total fits — union/mutual kNN at 3/6/12 neighbors and full RBF at sigma .08/.25/1.0 — with raw-coordinate k-means alongside, two clusters and 12 starts fixed, least embedding inertia choosing only a k-means restart, and generator labels never choosing a graph or fit. The failures stay visible: moons union-3 reaches ARI 1.000 with eigengap .003156 while moons RBF sigma 1.0 gives ARI .242035 with the larger eigengap .350982 — a larger gap is not agreement with the intended classes, and raw-coordinate k-means gives the same .242035; rings union-6 reaches ARI 1.000 while rings union-12 gives ARI -.010195, so adding edges does not promise a better grouping; and moons mutual-3 has four disconnected components, so its two-dimensional truncation of a four-dimensional zero eigenspace is not unique and that fit is refused, with no arbitrary cluster labels shown. An original seven-vertex weighted graph has only 63 distinct nonempty binary cuts after fixing one cluster label; independent rational enumeration finds the optimum 3450/4181 = .8251614446304711, while the archived spectral relaxation plus k-means returns .860832137733142 — a worse cut, with both partitions and every weight visible, and no exact-optimality claim for the larger fixtures. V0.2 adds full degree-response derivatives and a basis-invariant projector tangent: a fixed undirected edge edit changes both endpoint degrees and every normalized incident edge, and manual differentiation retains those terms; the bottom-two spectral projector P = U Uᵀ has a basis-invariant derivative using only retained-to-excluded denominators, with internal repeated eigenvalues allowed and repeated eigenvalues crossing the retained boundary refused without an epsilon repair. Six original small graphs include four smooth base cases and 28 predeclared finite states, one reaching a five-cycle boundary tie; a six-cycle and an isolated vertex are refused at the base, while the two disconnected triangles retain their full zero eigenspace and have a valid one-sided graph-domain response when a missing bridge is added. Every actual finite eigensolve and 12-start k-means result is archived, not predicted from the derivative; shared-scale heatmaps compare actual ΔP with tP′; the archived tiny-step assignment change is explicitly a rounding-ambiguity control — distinct tested partitions have numerically equal inertia — not a unique-optimum, label-derivative, robustness-certificate or stability-rate claim. New independent checks include 83 normalized-Laplacian edge finite differences, 42 exact Fraction cut derivatives, analytic and repeated-block projector controls, basis rotations including an archived eigenspace rotation, one-sided Taylor error ratios and derivative-identity residuals. 33 Python tests include the sensitivity controls above and cover 528 independent Fraction-distance graph references, 558 rational cut references, analytic cycle spectra and energy identities, component/zero-degree/boundary-tie refusals, invariant subspace rotations, every fresh graph fit, source/degree/eigenvector/label/score mutations and strict CLI behavior; eigenspace comparisons use projectors, not signs. Nine Node/real-Chrome tests include the new sensitivity view and cover 18 reconstructed graphs, 4,896 scalar eigenpair-row equations, all fitted/raw cuts, separate BigInt enumeration of 63 tiny-graph cuts, exact browser dataset exports, 1,728 actual browser point/graph states, refusal clearing, physical keyboard changes that leave fit witnesses unchanged, clipboard success/failure, real JSON Blob downloads and 320/390/1280 layouts with 44-pixel controls. Scalar JavaScript checks 146 full-base eigenpair equations and 1,022 matrix row cells, and real Chrome visits 168 new case/step/row states. The installed wheel reproduces outside the checkout, importing all six modules, executing nine commands, refitting all 18 graphs, auditing fresh and archived reports and verifying four archived web exports byte-for-byte, including fresh and archived sensitivity audits; CI runs Python 3.10-3.13, package reproduction and real Chrome. These are dense full-data synthetic illustrations, not held-out prediction, exact discrete optimization, optimal-graph selection or calibrated uncertainty; eigenvector signs are arbitrary, zero-degree vertices are not silently connected, and numerical eigenpair residuals are checks, not formal proofs. Original synthetic code and data are MIT licensed; the method follows Ng, Jordan and Weiss (2001), with von Luxburg's tutorial and explicit NumPy primitive attribution in the method note. [Source](https://github.com/midya8/spectrallab). [Live graph inspector](https://midya8.github.io/spectrallab/). [Sensitivity inspector](https://midya8.github.io/spectrallab/sensitivity.html). [Equations and limits](https://github.com/midya8/spectrallab/blob/main/METHOD.md).
- **ForestLab** - Manual binary CART forests with exact integer split ordering and a live membership inspector, built from scratch — it keeps every bootstrap count visible instead of letting a vote fraction hide which trees saw the observation: binary CART split costs are compared by arbitrary-precision integer cross products, checked by sixty independent Fraction designs and a separate BigInt complete-mask enumerator, with explicit conventions for lower-observed thresholds, feature/cut ties, minimum leaves, pure nodes and nonpositive gains — exact node ordering is not a claim of a globally optimal tree. Eight 31-tree forests retain all 248 bootstrap vectors and 7,102 tree nodes; the browser recomputes all 31 paths and exposes every in-bag count for a stored training group, edited inputs drop group membership immediately, and an OOB score uses only trees with zero draws of that group — zero eligible trees means no prediction, never the full-forest result. The original licensed UCI file has 1,372 numeric records but only 1,348 unique four-feature vectors; exact copies are collapsed and kept together before the fixed 808/270/270 train/validation/test split, with original row IDs auditable, and no physical specimen identifier is available, so near-duplicates and shared specimens are not ruled out. Each model family selects by validation errors, then Brier score, then ID before test labels are read; all eight forests, four single trees and seven controls remain visible, and the selected forest makes 3/270 test errors against the stronger selected 5NN control at 0/270 and the selected single tree at 8/270 — one historical split, not a claim of zero future error or a test of real authentication, and the stronger control stays visible. 39 Python and 12 Node/real-Chrome tests check fresh fitting, source/group/split provenance, all bootstrap vectors, complete node witnesses, train-only standardization and logistic gradients; test-label poisoning leaves fits and selections unchanged, and source, split, bootstrap, gain, metric and selection mutations are rejected. An independent scalar JavaScript implementation checks 10,784 forest evaluations, 8,088 control scores and 6,464 OOB records; Chrome drives all 992 native path references and every model/partition state, physical-keyboard membership changes, invalid-input clearing/recovery, clipboard success/failure, actual JSON Blob download and 320/390/1280-pixel layouts; the installed ten-module package runs all ten commands, refits all 19 models, audits them and reproduces the checked browser exports outside the checkout. V0.2 adds [joint permutation diagnostics](https://midya8.github.io/forestlab/permutation.html): all 15 nonempty feature blocks move through the same 16 fixed row bijections, with selected columns from the same donor and recipient labels and remaining features in place; both frozen models, the selected forest and the stronger selected 5NN, retain all 240 cases and 129,600 model-row prediction counts, with every repeat including negative error changes visible. Baseline validation errors are 4/270 and 1/270, distinct from the unchanged test errors 3/270 and 0/270; no model is refitted, and repeat ranges are not confidence intervals. An exact three-row, six-bijection control exposes off-support singleton hybrids. The added diagnostic has independent scalar-JavaScript predictions for all 129,600 model-row evaluations, exact Fraction metric controls, full fresh regeneration, fit/map/count mutation rejection, train/test-label poisoning and 1,230 actual browser states; outside-checkout reproduction regenerates all 240 cases, audits them and compares seven archive/loader exports exactly. Classes stay the source's labels 0 and 1, not an invented genuine/forged mapping; these are historical precomputed image descriptors, not an image-upload classifier or a currency detector, and hard votes, leaf fractions and neighbor fractions are not calibrated probabilities, confidence intervals or conformal sets. OOB is a training-sample diagnostic, not the held-out full-forest test or independent specimen validation, and paths are not causal explanations. Original data: Lohweg (2012), UCI Banknote Authentication, DOI 10.24432/C55P57, CC BY 4.0; original bytes are retained in lossless checked text encoding, with exact-copy grouping and partitioning as this project's transformations, and no source images were copied; code is MIT, and established CART/forest methods are credited in the method note, where attribution is not endorsement. [Repository](https://github.com/midya8/forestlab). [Live inspector](https://midya8.github.io/forestlab/). [Method, sources and limits](https://github.com/midya8/forestlab/blob/main/METHOD.md). The v0.3 leaf-collision inspector exposes all-tree similarity as an explicit Gram matrix, with exact integer/BigInt sum-of-squares witnesses, and a separate jointly-OOB view keeps pair-specific tree counts with undefined comparisons left undefined: a constructed negative quadratic form (−2) and a triangle failure show why OOB normalization is not a universal kernel guarantee. All 512 small partition controls, 5,760 native/scalar-JS matrix entries, 1,152 actual pair states and 13,824 heatmap colors are checked; proximity is the mean of per-tree leaf collisions (P=ZZᵀ/T), squared feature-space distance is 2(1−P), and the three-row control gives cᵀP_oob c=−2 for c=(1,−2,1) against an all-tree form of 10/3. Frozen supervised model geometry, not semantic similarity, causal influence, uncertainty or a new score. [Geometry derivation and limits](https://github.com/midya8/forestlab/blob/main/PROXIMITY.md).
- **RatingLab** - Observed-only rating factorization with manual NumPy alternating ridge solves, built from scratch — it keeps the observed edges visible instead of filling unobserved pairs with zeros: rating 0 is an actual observation here, and fitting an unobserved pair as zero would invent evidence, so every joint bias-and-factor block is derived as a regularized linear system with the training mean fixed, the sum-squared objective applying the regularizer once per parameter rather than once per observation. Each user and item update checks its local objective decrease, with half-pass accounting telescoping to the global objective, and full gradients are kept separate from the residual of the last solved block; an all-zero-factor stationary control has objective 4 while an explicit feasible factorization has objective .4, so a zero gradient is not a global-minimum certificate. On 1,161 distinct user-item pairs from 138 users and 130 items, a frozen observed-edge split uses 696 training, 232 validation and 233 test rows, with all held-out entities warm; eight rank-2/rank-4 factor fits use two penalties and two seeds, and all eight exhaust their stationarity budget, while a separate bias-only family selects among three penalties with the same validation RMSE rule, and test rows are evaluated only after both selections, without refitting. The selected factor fit has test RMSE .656456774 against .656175974 for the selected bias-only control, with training mean .758069714 — the bias control stays visible even though it slightly wins, and this is one historical split, not a significance or general-superiority claim; the source rows have no timestamps, so the split is not chronological, and synthetic cold-entity tests do not establish measured cold-start performance. Sixty exact Fraction Gaussian-elimination references check the ridge solutions, finite differences check every coordinate of a tiny complete model gradient, all eleven saved candidates are fitted again, and poisoning every test label must leave fitting, histories and selection unchanged; an independent scalar JavaScript implementation checks 12,771 predictions against native references, and real Chrome exercises eleven models, 33 visible pairs, physical keyboard rotation, normal matrices, invalid-input recovery, clipboard success/failure, a JSON Blob download and 320/390/1280-pixel layouts. A shared orthogonal rotation preserves predictions and regularization while changing coordinate products, so those products are algebraic terms, not identified tastes, semantic attributes or causal importance, and the browser's 130-item strip keeps missing observations empty rather than pretending to be a validated recommendation ranking. The v0.2 conditional-response extension separates saved scores, a freshly re-solved unmodified ridge block and its finite rating edit or row deletion, exposing leverage, the positive deletion denominator, query derivatives and all-opposite-index response curves; sixty additional exact rational design systems, 2,784 direct deleted-block refits and 576 native/scalar-JavaScript witnesses check the formulas, with original factors and scores unchanged. The two conditional normal systems remain positive definite with λ > 0, including deletion of a block's sole observation; a target edit has exactly linear finite response within this quadratic problem, and Sherman-Morrison gives a deletion update scaled by the original conditional residual and 1 − h, with a bound 1 − h ≥ λ/(λ + ‖a‖²) following from the deleted system's ridge floor, while numerical real-data readouts are not rational or interval certificates. The saved user block may no longer be its conditional minimizer after the final item pass, and that movement is reported separately before any edit; the inspector's three displayed scores prevent a changed conditioning baseline from being mislabeled as an observation's effect, and a deleted-row residual is algebra, not a valid full-model cross-validation estimate, since the original opposite factors and training mean still encode the row. The mean and opposite factors remain frozen and retain information from the row: this is not full-model leave-one-out validation, causal influence, data unlearning or an uncertainty bound, and no original model, metric, split, source row or original figure is modified. 37 Python and 14 Node/real-Chrome tests cover 60 independent exact rational ridge references, eleven fresh refits, test-label poisoning and 12,771 scalar/native predictions, with a ten-module installed wheel with eight main CLI commands and two single-row conditional-response module commands, reproducing all ten modules including the package root, eleven fits and both full audits outside the source checkout. Only the licensed rating CSV is bundled; consumer profiles, restaurant locations, names and demographics are not used, source identifiers become stable indices rather than features, and MovieLens was considered but not redistributed because of its license terms. Historical UCI restaurant and consumer data are CC BY 4.0; code is MIT, and missing pairs are not negatives, while observed-rating RMSE is not top-N relevance, missing-pair accuracy or calibrated certainty. [Original UCI dataset](https://archive.ics.uci.edu/dataset/232/restaurant+consumer+data). The v0.3 joint-batch inspector changes or removes multiple original training rows within one frozen block. Target edits add; joint deletions solve a coupled Schur system and generally differ from a sum of separate-original-baseline deletions. It shows the full source-bound problem, conservative positive-ridge bound, numerical condition/refusal checks and all-query joint/separate curves. Independent checks add 320 rational direct-system cases, 128 real augmented-least-squares cases and 320 complete native/browser Cholesky responses plus 4 controls. 37 Python and 14 Node/actual-Chrome tests, ten-module wheel and eight main CLI plus two single-row module commands. Original fits, scores and stronger bias control stay unchanged. This remains a conditional calculation, not full ALS retraining, cross-validation, causal attribution, uncertainty or data unlearning. - **MarginLab** - Manual linear/RBF soft-margin SVM learning with replayable pair updates and editable 30-feature scores, built from scratch — it keeps the constraints visible instead of letting a solver's claim stand alone: the original two-variable dual solver handles linear and RBF kernels with exact bounded line maxima and zero-curvature endpoints, and every accepted pair retains its indices, changes, curvature and objective gain, with a separate replay checking all constraints, endpoints and the objective telescope. Training-only scaling, a frozen 341/113/115 split and validation-only selection prevent test-based selection; the full-30D nearest-neighbor control is not weakened to the displayed two dimensions, and all eight fits remain visible, including two with a better test score than the validation choice. A tiny independent Fraction active-face KKT oracle checks 60 optima without calling the pair solver, and full-data stopping remains numerical, not an exact-rational certificate. Four separable one-dimensional points reach 100% training accuracy after one pair update while the optimization gap is still 2/3; opposite labels at an identical input give zero pair curvature and require endpoint comparison; duplicate same-label inputs have nonunique support coefficients at the same decision function — correct labels, unique causal importance and a solved optimization are different claims. The v0.2 extension repairs the saved binary64 dual equality before proving exact rational weak-duality bounds for all four represented linear training problems: the original saved floating-point coefficients have nonzero exact signed dual mass, so their represented binary64 inputs are converted to Fractions, clipped to the box, and coefficients in the excess class reduced until the equality is exactly zero, recording the original residual and total movement without changing the original model. A separate exact feasible primal and repaired feasible dual enclose each represented linear training optimum, with approximate gap readouts for C=.01,.1,1,10 of 7.97e-8, 1.42e-6, 2.90e-5 and 3.23e-4 — the full canonical objective ratios, not those decimals, are the bounds. Two independently implemented readers, Python Fraction and browser BigInt, check all constraints, objectives and repair accounting without importing the optimizer; sixty additional active-face exact optima must lie inside the produced intervals, and the browser also checks all four full proofs against every original standardized input, label, C, coefficient and bias. A changed inactive row is an explicit control: its proof can remain mathematically valid for its changed declared problem while source matching rejects it, so proof validity does not authenticate data or authorship. 36 Python and 15 Node/real-Chrome tests cover 120 exhaustive 1-5D corner problems, all 256 extremal scores recomputed from the exact original dual sum, 128 complete native/browser responses and 128 actual Chrome UI combinations, plus the 60 exact active-face optima, four full proofs and eight fresh fits, with four Python CI versions and an 11-module/nine-command installed wheel plus the box exporter reproducing all eight fresh fits and full saved/fresh pair audits; the browser checks all 4,552 native/scalar-JavaScript scores, every feature, 24 UI model/example states, physical keyboard input, blocked/successful clipboard, actual JSON Blob download and 320/390/1280 layouts including horizontal-table reachability. The plot is a two-feature slice with the other 28 features at their training means, not a projection, and algebraic score terms are not causal effects. Original UCI Wisconsin Diagnostic data is CC BY 4.0 with exact source hashes; source metadata is losslessly encoded to preserve its original missing final newline. This is a bound for the represented standardized linear problem, not exact real-number standardization, RBF kernels, generalization error, clinical reliability or a claim that an exact optimum was attained; the original numerical inspector stays numerical, with no prediction, score, split, pair history or original figure changed. Educational historical data, not clinical validation, calibrated certainty, generalization bounds or source authentication. [Live 30-feature inspector](https://midya8.github.io/marginlab/). [Live exact linear proof inspector](https://midya8.github.io/marginlab/certificate.html). [Equations, source attribution and limits](https://github.com/midya8/marginlab/blob/main/METHOD.md). [Derivation, repair algorithm and limits](https://github.com/midya8/marginlab/blob/main/CERTIFICATE.md). The v0.3 extension adds exact finite original-unit input boxes: the frozen represented linear models define an exact affine function in original feature units, so coordinate signs yield its exact minimum and maximum on a finite rational box with complete corner vectors attaining each, both label claims use the existing +1-at-zero rule, and independent native Fraction and browser BigInt readers check all coordinate support equalities and scores, with the page separately reconstructing the affine function from every original saved coefficient, represented training input, mean, scale and bias, and valid changed-function controls keeping source identity separate from mathematics. Presets expand eight fixed historical queries by exact plain-decimal multiples of training-only scales — synthetic independent-coordinate sets, not measurement tolerances, domain-valid inputs or plausible causal interventions — and all 30 intervals can be edited, with the declared center required to remain inside and edits, malformed inputs and source mismatches clearing old results and blocking export; approximate plots include zero and stay separate from the full exact witness fractions and unchanged scalar floating center scores. New checks add 120 exhaustive 1-5D corner problems, all 256 extremal scores independently recomputed from the exact original dual sum, 128 complete native/browser responses and 128 actual Chrome UI combinations, covering zero coefficients/nonunique corners, asymmetric intervals, point boxes, ties, strict schemas, stale/invalid states, physical keyboard, blocked/successful copy, actual JSON Blob and mobile layout; four bounded artifact chunks and a small source-affine declaration reproduce without fitting, while the existing 60 exact active-face optima, full pair replay, training-objective proofs and holdout-poisoning checks remain. Original fits, holdout scores, split and exact training-objective proofs are unchanged; the exact rational affine function is not a bound on rounded production inference or exact real-number standardization, and no RBF, clinical, probability, causal, generalization, domain-validity or authenticity claim is made.
