- **MixtureLab** - Manual Gaussian-mixture EM on real, licensed chemistry, built from scratch — it keeps the likelihood honest instead of letting a bigger model claim a win: on UCI Wine (Aeberhard & Forina 1992), 178 chemical analyses with 13 features, the loader strips the cultivar target before returning features, so no labels enter splitting, fitting or scoring; a fixed label-free split uses 106 training, 36 validation and 36 test rows, scaling is training-only, both covariance families, k in {1,2,3,5} and three fixed seeds yield 24 fits, with a covariance floor of 0.05 in squared standardized units. Training likelihood chooses each restart, validation NLL chooses the configuration, and test scores open afterward without a validation refit, with all candidates staying visible: the validation pick, Diagonal k=5 seed 11, scores 13.40992 / 13.45753 / 15.34782 (train/validation/test NLL); the fitted-Gaussian control, Full k=1 seed 11, scores 14.40496 / 14.99046 / 15.72686; and the best-training configuration, Full k=5 seed 23, scores 10.20168 / 17.95955 / 18.47913 — more capacity improves training density here while worsening test density, and this one small historical split does not show general superiority or statistical significance. With a fixed covariance constraint C >= 0.05I, the full-covariance M-step clips weighted scatter eigenvalues; adding 0.05I is a different operation and generally does not solve that subproblem. The method derives the precision-space KKT conditions and checks them numerically on 100 random scatters, with 40 independent analytic 2D clipping references. Every EM update keeps the old posterior and records mean likelihood gain = complete-data Q gain + posterior KL; all initial/final models, complete scalar histories and final before/after transition witnesses are retained. All 24 fits meet the stated likelihood-increment tolerance — that is not a stationary-point test or a global optimum certificate — and zero numerical component mass fails explicitly, with no hidden reseeding. A separate scalar JavaScript Cholesky implementation recomputes density and every component responsibility for arbitrary edited 13-feature vectors; training-only PCA supplies a view, not the fit, and its ellipses are 90% probability contours of the model's 2D Gaussian marginals, not full-dimensional decision regions or measured coverage. The [live 13-feature density inspector](https://midya8.github.io/mixturelab/) exposes all eight model choices, all 24 restart summaries, the complete current-fit trajectory, JSON witness copy/download and clear invalid-input errors. The far-input control shows how a nearly certain within-model posterior can accompany very low density. 36 Python tests and eight Node/real-Chrome tests cover independent scalar/2D Gaussian likelihoods, weighted moments, precision-space KKT checks, analytic covariance clipping, EM identities, holdout-poisoning isolation, original-unit density transformations, all eight models x 178 source rows (1,424 native/browser evaluations), every responsibility, edited inputs, physical keyboard behavior, copy/download and 320/390/1280 layouts; a built wheel imports ten modules and runs all seven CLI commands outside the checkout, including all 24 fresh fits and saved/fresh audits. The v0.2 fixed-model geometry extension adds a [live gradient, curvature and ascent inspector](https://midya8.github.io/mixturelab/geometry.html) computing all 13 input-score coordinates, the complete 13x13 Hessian, the kx13 component-posterior Jacobian and original-unit transforms — fixed-model input derivatives, not parameter training or causal feature importance — with a chosen finite coordinate change compared against first- and second-order predictions. A normalized-gradient line search accepts only an actual log-density gain at least 0.0001 times step length times gradient norm, retains every accepted point and stops explicitly on norm, budget or line-search exhaustion; eight traces use the same already-public input at budget 128, where the four diagonal models and full k=1 reach the norm criterion while full k=2,3,5 remain budget-exhausted, and no mode is certified. Two equally weighted unit Gaussians at -2 and +2 have zero input gradient and curvature +3 at zero; the routine stops there at a strict minimum, visibly demonstrating why its norm rule is not a mode certificate. Independent checks include 312 all-coordinate finite-difference probes, every posterior-Jacobian and Hessian column, 50 scalar directional second differences, affine/rotation/permutation checks, 40 complete native/browser geometry cases and eight trace references; new tests recreate only the two new reference artifacts, checking every nested numeric value with explicit tolerance and preserving keys, shapes, statuses and counts exactly. All original models, experiment report, source data, split, scales, browser references, browser dataset and score figure remain byte-for-byte unchanged. No cultivar prediction, wine-quality score, calibrated uncertainty, identified latent cause, global mixture optimum, floor/split sensitivity guarantee or population-wide conclusion. Data are CC BY 4.0; code is MIT. [Derivation, frozen protocol and source ledger](https://github.com/midya8/mixturelab/blob/main/METHOD.md). [Derivative and search derivation](https://github.com/midya8/mixturelab/blob/main/GEOMETRY.md). The v0.3 frozen conditional inference extension adds a [live observed-coordinate conditional inspector](https://midya8.github.io/mixturelab/conditional.html) that conditions any observed-coordinate subset under the same frozen model: each component uses observed-marginal evidence, a Schur-complement conditional mean/covariance and log-space posterior weights, with complete within/between covariance, raw-unit Jacobians and zero-dimensional limits in the numerical witness. Native Cholesky solves and scalar browser loops add no ridge or conditional-covariance clipping, and numerical breakdown is refused rather than repaired. The mean can lie in a low-density gap: a separate diagonal-mixture control increases target variance from 1.09 to 25.1 by changing posterior component weights while each component target variance stays 0.1. 45 Python and eleven Node/Chrome tests include 100 separate precision-block Gaussian oracles and direct scalar Gaussian formulas, all 5,696 original model/row/four-mask density factorizations and moment decompositions, 160 complete native/scalar-JS references and all 160 through the UI. Twelve-module/nine-command installed reproduction retains all 24 original fresh refits and audits. Analytical masks on complete source data, not real missingness validation, causal intervention, calibrated intervals or new accuracy. [Conditional algebra and sources](https://github.com/midya8/mixturelab/blob/main/CONDITIONAL.md).
- **PartsLab** - Nonnegative parts learning with manual NumPy factorization, built from scratch — it keeps the strong controls in view instead of letting the factorization grade itself: six fits on all 3,823 original UCI training digits without labels, ranks 8/16/32 with seeds 11/23 and 100 outer / 3 inner coordinate sweeps, lowest reencoded training MSE selecting the restart within each rank; training-only restart selection and a frozen model/protocol hash verified before evaluation opens the 1,797-row official holdout. Rank-matched signed PCA and SVD beat NMF on test MSE at every fixed rank — rank 8: 0.0309683 vs 0.0251267/0.0257206; rank 16: 0.0163996 vs 0.0118776/0.0119639; rank 32: 0.0046791 vs 0.0027689/0.0027681 — against a training-mean baseline of 0.0735163, so no NMF-superiority claim is made. All six joint fits exhaust their fixed stationarity budget rather than meeting joint stationarity tolerance, so decreasing loss is not convergence; frozen-basis NNLS is convex and all official test rows meet the numerical per-row KKT threshold in every saved run, while an arbitrary input may exhaust 1,000 sweeps and is marked unfinished. A plain multiplicative-update control with target [1,1], identity basis and initial coefficients [1,0] stays at loss 0.5 with a blocked gradient -1 where coordinate minimization reaches zero, isolating zero-locking; positive rescaling, shared permutations and duplicate basis rows show additive parts are not unique semantic explanations. 38 Python and eleven Node/real-Chrome tests include independent active-set oracles, finite-difference gradients, 35 support-enumerated NNLS optima, full test-loader-prohibited refits and exact SVG regeneration, with native and browser decompositions agreeing on all 120 model/example pairs and real Chrome checking all 2,048 pixels in the 32-part contribution grid; a ten-module/ten-CLI installed wheel reproduces all six fits and held-out scores. A [live parts inspector](https://midya8.github.io/partslab/) solves actual new nonnegative coefficients for editable pixels in scalar JavaScript, showing individual contributions, a separately rescaled basis view, numerical KKT status and unclipped reconstruction errors. The v0.2 fixed-basis certificate adds exact binary64 primal/dual arithmetic, outward-rounded objective intervals and portable JSON proofs with an independent Python Fraction verifier: eighty exact rational active-set optima and all 120 cross-language witness references are checked, the browser verifies its own candidates including zero-sweep unfinished solves and accepts valid pasted proofs while rejecting tampered ones; the bounds are half squared error rather than per-pixel MSE, a residual-shift bound can be loose, and even zero gap does not imply unique coefficients. Data are Alpaydin & Kaynak (1998), UCI Optical Recognition of Handwritten Digits, DOI 10.24432/C50P49, CC BY 4.0; code is MIT. [Equations, protocol and limits](https://github.com/midya8/partslab/blob/main/METHOD.md). [Certificate proof and limits](https://github.com/midya8/partslab/blob/main/CERTIFICATE.md). The v0.3 exact fixed-face response adds an [exact fixed-face pixel inspector](https://midya8.github.io/partslab/face.html): nominate a face from the numeric NNLS result, re-solve it exactly, and intersect every affine KKT inequality with the pixel domain; all endpoint ties, singular refusals, singleton faces and duplicate-basis nonuniqueness stay explicit. 38 Python and eleven Node/Chrome tests cover 200 independently enumerated tiny face candidates, all 120 stored nominations, 54 complete native/BigInt/Worker witnesses and 10,368 endpoint pixel colors; ten modules/ten commands with all six fresh fits reproduced. Original data, models and scores remain unchanged. One candidate interval, not a full path, unique support, robustness or joint-NMF optimum. [Math and limits](https://github.com/midya8/partslab/blob/main/FACE.md).
- **ClusterLab** - Real-data unsupervised clustering with manual k-means++/Lloyd updates, built from scratch — it makes the two classic k-means mistakes hard to miss: forty complete restart histories stay visible, learned centroids show mixed true-digit membership, and the browser recomputes membership for editable digits instead of replaying saved predictions. Four fixed arms — raw geometry with k-means++ starts, raw geometry with uniform starts, fitting-only PCA16 and whitened PCA16 — each choose minimum fitting inertia among seeds 100–109, never a test-label score; all 3,823 original UCI training digits fit without labels and the 1,797-row official holdout is audited with permutation-invariant scores and all-row silhouettes, giving holdout ARI .662690/.665149/.669707/.665596 across the four arms. Careful seeding has slightly lower raw fitting inertia (9682.7571 vs uniform 9683.1241) but uniform has slightly higher test ARI, so no general seeding win is claimed; PCA and whitening change the distance metric, so cross-geometry inertia/silhouette differences are descriptive and cannot be ranked as one unchanged objective. A [live clustering inspector](https://midya8.github.io/clusterlab/) projects edited 8x8 inputs and computes distances to all ten learned centers locally, with keyboard controls, a centroid atlas, held-out composition, all restart curves and JSON copy/download; the first twenty original test rows are included by index, not success. 48 Python tests plus thirteen Node/real-Chrome tests cover independent scalar objective and D² sampling references, pair/null-permutation ARI, joint-entropy NMI, all-pair silhouette, PCA eigenspectrum and whitening checks, a full 40-fit reproduction with test-loader prohibition, frozen model/protocol hashes verified before evaluation opens the test file, and all 80 public model/example pairs, with a ten-module/six-CLI installed wheel and CI on Python 3.10–3.13. The deliberate failure case shows why descent is not a global certificate: for [0,1,4,5,9,10] with k=2, initialization [0,1] converges to inertia 26.5 while exhaustive exact-rational enumeration of all 31 nonempty binary partitions gives the global optimum 17.5. The v0.2 restart inspector exposes all forty saved models, 180 distinct within-arm restart comparisons and forty self-pairs. A manual integer Hungarian solver supplies optimal center permutations and primal/dual witnesses, independently checked against subset-DP optima for all 220 matrices; browser BigInt checks those exact saved rounded-cost certificates, with 800 native/scalar-JavaScript inference pairs and all 400 directed pair controls tested. In raw k-means++, seeds 106 and 107 disagree most on fitting partitions (ARI .556972), while seed 107 remains the original lowest-inertia choice; geometric matching can visibly disagree with pointwise assignments, and same-data initialization sensitivity is not sampling stability or useful-class discovery. Original fits and holdout scores remain unchanged. [Open the live restart inspector](https://midya8.github.io/clusterlab/stability.html). [Restart matching method](https://github.com/midya8/clusterlab/blob/main/STABILITY.md). The v0.3 ambiguity audit adds 2,200 exact selected-edge exclusion witnesses, best-distinct permutations and optimal-matching counts. Independent full-cost dynamic programs check all 220 gaps and counts; browser BigInt verifies all 4,000 oriented exclusions. The matching is unique in every saved real matrix, while tied and unused-tight-edge controls expose why an optimum alone proves neither uniqueness nor a large margin. A strict sufficient cost-entry perturbation bound is not a sharp radius, pixel robustness or statistical uncertainty; the live inspector now shows exact optimal-permutation counts, the cheapest distinct permutation, every selected-edge exclusion and a smallest-gap control, and all ten exclusion certificates together cover every possible replacement without adding their overlapping solution counts. All original fits, scores and earlier matching witnesses remain unchanged. [Uniqueness and gap proof](https://github.com/midya8/clusterlab/blob/main/AMBIGUITY.md). Data are Alpaydin & Kaynak (1998), UCI Optical Recognition of Handwritten Digits, DOI 10.24432/C50P49, CC BY 4.0; code is MIT. Cluster IDs are groups, not digit labels; distances are not probabilities — no classifier accuracy, correct-k claim, sampling-stability estimate or global-optimality certificate is made.
- **DenoiseLab** - Learned masked-digit reconstruction with manual NumPy backprop, built from scratch — it keeps the strong controls in view instead of letting the network grade itself: on all 1,797 official UCI test digits, with error measured only on missing pixels in units normalized from [0,16] to [0,1], nearest neighbor (0.0312412) and PCA ridge (0.0317004) both beat the neural net (0.0353774) at 16 scattered missing pixels, while the network is lower at 32 (0.0340515 against PCA 0.0410443 and neighbor 0.0363968); at 48 scattered pixels the three are nearly tied (neural 0.0546866, PCA 0.0547961, neighbor 0.0549125) and on a 4-by-4 block the network leads (0.0649021 against neighbor 0.0650337 and PCA 0.0723497), with the pixel-mean baseline far behind in every case — no universal ranking is inferred. The network takes 64 erased values plus 64 missing flags through tanh widths 64, 16, 64 and 64 sigmoid outputs, 14,544 learned parameters, with every derivative written out including hidden-only MSE and weight L2 and explicit Adam; training hides exactly 32 randomly selected pixels per image, refreshed on every presentation, and epoch candidates 10, 30 and 60 are selected on validation alone, with 60 winning as validation hidden-pixel MSE falls from 0.1797185 at initialization to 0.0320862. The official 3,823 training / 1,797 test split stays intact, with a deterministic class-stratified feature-hash split giving 3,062 fitting / 761 validation rows and no exact feature duplicates across partitions. The PCA control fits its mean/SVD basis on fitting rows only, selects rank 16 and ridge 0.1 from rank 8/16/32 and ridge .001/.01/.1/1 on the same validation masks, and infers coefficients from observed coordinates only with ridge and output clipping — not a deliberately weak zero-filled projection — while nearest neighbor scores squared error on visible pixels only with lowest fitting index on ties, and labels are used for the split, never fitting or prediction. A [live reconstruction inspector](https://midya8.github.io/denoiselab/) recomputes the neural denoiser, the observed-pixel PCA ridge solve, the exact fitting-example neighbor and the pixel mean in the browser when arbitrary pixels are erased, with independent scalar JavaScript inference. 27 Python tests plus four Node/real-Chrome tests cover 200 independent gradient probes, complete refit with a test-loader prohibition, full evaluation reproduction, all 40 public inference examples and responsive keyboard/copy/download controls, with a seven-module/four-CLI installed wheel and CI refitting and evaluating the installed package outside the checkout. Data are Alpaydin & Kaynak (1998), UCI Optical Recognition of Handwritten Digits, DOI 10.24432/C50P49, CC BY 4.0; code is MIT. One historical dataset, small images, one model/seed/budget and fixed artificial missingness — masked reconstruction error does not establish semantic preservation, calibrated uncertainty, image generation, clinical restoration or behavior on naturally missing observations, and this is a standalone reconstruction experiment, not DigitLab's classifier/attack study or LatentLab's synthetic variational density model. The v0.2 fixed-mask response extension derives input response from the fixed parameters — full chain-rule matrices through the three tanh layers and sigmoid for the network, and an observed-only linear-system derivative for PCA — with actual edited reconstructions kept separate from local tangent predictions, compared on shared-scale signed heatmaps, and hidden targets staying erased. Saturated PCA rows have zero local response, and nonconstant clipping-boundary rows are null rather than an invented derivative; nearest-neighbor response retains the old and new retrieved indices and a frozen-original-neighbor image, with a scalar switch control. Complete unbounded tangents and residuals can be copied or downloaded; only the rendered image clamps them. The suite grows to 35 Python and eight Node tests, covering derivative checks including independent finite differences and augmented least squares, 120 full cross-language responses, 480 browser views and 8,192 heatmap pixel checks, with an eight-module/five-command installed reproduction, and the original models, holdout scores and stronger winning controls stay unchanged. [Equations, independent checks and source ledger](https://github.com/midya8/denoiselab/blob/main/RESPONSE.md). V0.3 adds exact scalar retrieval: for a single observed-pixel edit, the complete retrieval path over every open interval plus the complete endpoint tie, with Fraction/BigInt witnesses, a separate intended-source verifier, all-row affine dominance, and coincident rows and point-only winners visible. The suite grows to 44 Python and eleven Node tests, covering 5,344 independent rational oracle queries, 40 complete cross-language paths, 160 browser queries and 30,720 actual canvas colors, with a nine-module/seven-command installed reproduction. The guarantee is for one edited pixel with the other inputs and the mask fixed — not a multicoordinate radius or binary64 tie guarantee. [Scalar paths](https://midya8.github.io/denoiselab/path.html). V0.4 extends the retrieval map to two observed values: the [two-pixel retrieval map](https://midya8.github.io/denoiselab/plane.html) shows exact rational closed minimum cells over two observed values — area, segment-only and point-only cells, with explicitly proved empty regions — with Fraction/BigInt witnesses retaining all 3,062 original fitting-row coefficients. A source-matched verifier checks supporting halfplanes, maximal lower-dimensional cells and nonnegative infeasibility combinations without rerunning clipping, and shared boundaries use complete all-row exact tie queries rather than an interior representative. The suite grows to 52 Python and fourteen Node/Chrome tests, including independent boundary-pair enumeration of 238 small-source cells and 2,110 rational queries, forty complete cross-language maps, 160 browser queries and 30,720 actual canvas colors, with a ten-module/nine-command installed reproduction. The map is a fixed two-coordinate slice only — not general robustness, causal recourse, uncertainty, better accuracy or binary64 tie equivalence. [Geometry, proof and limits](https://github.com/midya8/denoiselab/blob/main/PLANE.md).
- **TreeLab** - Inspectable tree learners and constrained counterfactual geometry, built from scratch in pure Python — it makes a frozen model's response to one feature, and the closest input reaching a target output band, inspectable as exact geometry, on one historical wine-quality table where every interval, witness and cost is an exact binary64 or rational value, not a claim about causal effects, plausible wines or new accuracy. The v0.6 extension searches the whole 32-tree ensemble at once instead of one tree: a best-first queue intersects all 32 unchanged trees, propagates forced branches through every member, and bounds the exact weighted-L1 objective from below by projecting onto the common feature box, with descendant output ranges pruning impossible target bands — so a finished search proves optimality or infeasibility under the supplied constraints, and a search that exhausts its budget is reported unfinished, never rounded up to an answer. The fixed 36-case audit crosses rows 1, 8 and 11, target bands [6,7] and [7,8], all-feature versus alcohol-only policies and 0/128/4,096 expansion budgets: at row 1, target [6,7], the alcohol-only search finishes with output 6.05953621031746 and cost 0.36290323, while the global all-feature search at 4,096 expansions remains unfinished with objective bounds [0.01838427, 0.36290323]; alcohol-only target [7,8] is proved infeasible, while the all-feature search retains a coarse expensive corner witness and a large gap, and no failure outcome is dropped. Optional seeds are checked against the bounds, immutable features and native full-ensemble inference, and the real demo uses already-known one-axis target witnesses as checked warm starts. The [live ensemble inspector](https://midya8.github.io/treelab/forest.html) displays every proof state, exact rational bounds, the full input and all 32 member outputs and leaf IDs, budget comparisons and source hashes. The 101 Python tests and five Node/real-Chrome tests cover independent tiny-forest exhaustive grids, budget-gap checks, every budget bound, native prediction re-scoring, subnormal midpoint and cost controls, strict requests, member inference and responsive inspector controls, with nine-module/nineteen-CLI installed-wheel checks; the method, proof scope and sources are in the source FOREST_SEARCH.md. Exact objective bounds apply to stored binary64 costs and single-input NumPy ensemble predictions — not exact training arithmetic, causal actionability, plausible chemistry, calibrated uncertainty or a scalable forest solver. The v0.5 extension adds constrained multi-feature model counterfactuals for the unchanged selected CART: find the closest input reaching a closed output band, subject to closed feature bounds, immutable indices and positive weighted L1 costs, by enumerating every target-output leaf, intersecting its path with the constraints and clamping the source into each feasible binary64 box. Strict right branches use nextafter, never an arbitrary epsilon; exact rational costs prevent false rounded ties and underflow; exact ties break by leaf ID; every candidate is re-routed. The fixed demo retains 36 requests — original test rows 1, 8 and 11, target bands [6,7], [7,8] and [9,9], all features, alcohol only, fixed alcohol and all fixed — with fitting-only min/max and reciprocal-range weights and no new fitting or scoring: for row 1, reaching [7,8] changes fixed acidity, volatile acidity and alcohol, with output 7.647058823529412 and cost about 0.27508758, alcohol alone cannot reach that band under the same bounds, and [9,9] has no target-output leaf. A [live constrained counterfactual explorer](https://midya8.github.io/treelab/counterfactual.html) displays every frozen request, feature delta, feasible candidate, full input/box/path and exact cost, with copy and local download including the complete request and report/model SHA-256 provenance. The 87 Python tests and four Node/real-Chrome tests include an independent exhaustive Cartesian-grid optimum check, exact and false rounded ties, subnormal costs, immutable and directional bounds, all real witnesses and responsive mobile controls, with eight-module/sixteen-CLI installed-wheel checks retaining all original data, models, historical controls and three audits; the method, CLI, proof scope and attribution are in the source COUNTERFACTUALS.md. This is a global weighted-L1 optimum over finite binary64 inputs for one stored CART under independent box constraints — not causal recourse, plausible chemistry, a feasible path, a bagging-ensemble optimum, calibrated uncertainty or a guarantee for outcomes in reality. The v0.4 extension is a live, frozen response explorer: the page bundles the complete source report and its SHA-256 without recomputing or rounding its intervals, witnesses or member outputs, with no API calls, accounts, analytics, remote inference or uploads, starting with row 1, the 32-tree ensemble and alcohol. Choose row, model and feature and inspect the full responsive step curve, exact interval inclusion, every member output and leaf ID and attainable min/max with complete input witnesses; the slider supports keyboard arrows, Previous/Next and a full interval list offer alternatives, and copy and local JSON download retain source row, model, feature, domain and interpretation warning, with denied clipboard access producing an explicit fallback rather than a false success. The 72 Python tests and three Node/real-Chrome tests include every one of the 66 UI slice selections and 1,028 segment witnesses and member displays, extrema, keyboard movement, copy success and denial, download names and expanded disclosures at 320/390/1280px, with chart labels reflowing for phones instead of shrinking desktop text; the live site passes the same real-Chrome checks and a byte-for-byte comparison of all five code/data/style files, and CI keeps Python 3.10-3.13 and installed-wheel seven-module/thirteen-CLI reproduction. All prior data, models, reports and three audit figures are unchanged; this is an interactive view of stored model-response geometry, not new inference, a new accuracy result, causality, calibrated uncertainty, realistic-wine guarantees or joint-feature robustness. The v0.3 extension computes exact one-feature response partitions: the first three historical test rows, original CSV indices 1, 8 and 11, all eleven features and both frozen models give 66 slices and 1,028 leaf segments, with domains from fitting-only min/max and rows 1 and 8 retained as exact copies rather than filtered for a more varied-looking demonstration. At non-axis splits, follow only the branch compatible with the fixed row; at axis splits, intersect the leaf's interval; combine all members' boundaries, average outputs on each common segment and return attainable extrema with complete input witnesses. Left routing is <=, upper endpoints are inclusive, the first lower endpoint is inclusive and later ones are exclusive, and open lower witnesses use math.nextafter, never an arbitrary epsilon. For the first test row, the complete alcohol slice has 3 selected-CART leaf segments and 50 ensemble segments, with model outputs ranging 5.31579-6.36283 for CART and 5.09926-6.54844 for bagging — model responses on a one-axis domain, not uncertainty intervals, causal improvements, possible-wine guarantees or multifeature robustness bounds, since correlated inputs may make synthetic feature combinations implausible. An opposite-stump control shows why member extrema are insufficient: each member can separately reach 10, but their mean is always 5, so the implementation uses common-axis leaf intersections, not the sum of separate extrema. The 70 tests include direct checks of all real-data segment boundaries, member leaf IDs and predictions, independent multidimensional leaf-box intersections, random small trees and ensembles, zero-width domains, repeated axes, nextafter boundaries, strict JSON/CLI and a no-refit complete report rerun, with the installed wheel exercising seven modules and all thirteen CLI modes and the audit SVG retaining its complete displayed curves, table data and regeneration checks. The v0.2 extension adds group-bootstrap bagging: draw 2,376 fitting-feature groups 2,376 times with replacement for each tree, then expand every drawn group with multiplicity, with grown members keeping minimum leaf size 5 and depth limit 20 and all feature columns available; seed 20261108 and the prefix grid [1,4,16,32] are fixed before the run, validation MSE alone selects 32 trees with no validation refit, bootstrap expanded row counts vary from 2,856 to 2,951, and leaf size counts multiplicity, not unique source rows. Out-of-bag excludes a row's entire exact-feature group, zero votes mean null, and prefixes 1/4/16/32 have 1,832/482/1/0 unsupported rows; each prefix's OOB MSE is on its own supported subset with a row-dependent number of trees, so it is not a fixed-set error curve or calibrated uncertainty interval, and a four-row duplicate witness demonstrates why row absence alone does not exclude an in-bag copy — which does not estimate real-data leakage magnitude. The 32-tree ensemble has test RMSE 0.705212 and MAE 0.545678 across 969 rows, versus same-fit least squares 0.734217/0.573791 and selected CART 0.775952/0.607582 — descriptive results on the reused historical test, not a fresh blind benchmark, statistical significance or causal isolation, and exact feature groups do not recover producer or batch identity. The complete ensemble JSON, all group draws and fitting/validation/test member outputs, per-row OOB votes and test predictions are saved, with 57 tests and a six-module/ten-CLI installed-wheel check covering full reproduction, independent traversal, group multiplicity, missing support, corruption guards and test-target mutation, and the original v0.1 data, models, report and audit figure unchanged. The protocol is fixed before held-out evaluation: the original UCI white-wine CSV is bundled without modification with its SHA-256, original names file and CC BY 4.0 attribution — 4,898 rows with 11 physicochemical features and an ordinal expert score, not a price, safety or causal target, and no official train/test split. Parsed feature values are serialized as binary64 float.hex strings, each vector hashed, the 3,961 unique groups sorted and the first floor(60%) assigned to fitting, the next through floor(80%) to validation and the rest to test — target-blind, source-row-order invariant and exact-feature-group disjoint, with target conflicts remaining together as a synthetic conflict control verifies, while near copies, unrecorded production batches and time-forward shift remain open. CART is fit with minimum leaf size 5 and maximum depth 20; greedy squared-error splits use every adjacent distinct feature boundary, left branches use <=, and weakest-link pruning saves every state's normalized fitting SSE, alpha, leaf count and removed node IDs, with exact floating ties cut together, ancestor cuts subsuming descendants and node statistics using math.fsum for reduction portability — the earlier dot-product prototype gave 307 states and the final stable statistics give 290 with the same selected predictions and test metrics. Validation MSE alone selects 19 leaves at alpha 0.00365165 with no refit; all 969 test predictions, the complete grown and selected model JSON and six first-test-row prediction routes are saved, the test is evaluated only after fitting, pruning and validation selection, and changing all test targets leaves the fitting, pruning path, validation scores, selected index and selected model unchanged. The control table keeps the stronger control in view: fitting mean 0.88943/0.67329, fitting median 0.90235/0.64499, grown CART with 425 leaves 0.88255/0.66363, validation-selected CART with 19 leaves 0.77595/0.60758 and ordinary least squares with 12 coefficients 0.73422/0.57379 (test RMSE/MAE), where the linear control uses fitting-only centering and scaling with an intercept solved by NumPy's least-squares routine and no tuned ridge parameter, squared error treats the ordinal scores as numeric, complexity units differ and this single historical split does not establish significance, future accuracy, a causal benefit of pruning or superiority over other model families. The independent checks are not self-agreement alone: forty all-threshold stump references recompute candidate SSEs directly, every subtree of 12 small depth-three fitted trees is enumerated with exact Fraction risks from training rows, production path and explicit-alpha prune objectives are checked at breakpoints, interval interiors, zero and a large alpha, and separate controls cover simultaneous weakest links, boundary inclusivity, adjacent binary64 threshold fallback, validation-score ties and target-scale alpha units, with full refit and report reproduction, all saved test predictions, group overlap, test-target mutation, linear normal equations, strict JSON corruption and complete SVG metadata, series and metrics checked, and the wheel installed outside the checkout exercising all five modules and all five CLI modes — demo, audit, fit, prune and predict — with prediction returning the complete branching route. The scope is stated plainly: greedy CART is not globally optimal tree learning, weakest-link pruning searches subtrees of one grown tree, alpha is score-squared per leaf because SSE is divided by the root fitting count, binary64 arithmetic, finite oracles and exact floating tie rules are not exact all-input certificates, a route explains this model's computation rather than feature causality or deletion effects, the original single-tree v0.1 has no missing or categorical data, sample weights, ensembles or production service, and out-of-range predictions are piecewise constant with no accuracy promise. Data and derived figures and metrics are Cortez et al. (2009), UCI Wine Quality, DOI 10.24432/C56S3T, CC BY 4.0; code is MIT; CART, weakest-link pruning and least squares are established methods, not claimed inventions, with no endorsement implied. The complete frozen ensemble is bundled as deterministic `bagging-model.json.gz` (gzip of the full JSON, zero timestamp) and CLI model loading accepts `.json.gz` as well as plain JSON — storage only, not trees, draws or predictions.

- **DigitLab** - Real UCI digit training in NumPy with manual gradients and a frozen official-test audit, built from scratch — it keeps the honest baseline in view: on 1,797 test digits, exact 1NN gets 1,760 correct, the tanh MLP (2,410 parameters) 1,734 and linear softmax (650 parameters) 1,698, all using the same 3,062 fitting examples, with test NLL 0.12096 and 0.16906 for the two trained models, so a stronger same-fit baseline is the result to beat, not a footnote. At normalized L-infinity radius 0.1, MLP correct counts fall to 898 under FGSM and 768 under a 20-step/three-start PGD candidate pool, versus 1,708 under one seeded random-sign control — continuous input attacks, not imperceptibility, certified robustness or an adaptive 1NN ranking. The v0.5 extension adds baseline-relative class-logit Integrated Gradients to a live model inspector for the unchanged UCI digit models: choose the class and a zero or fit-only mean reference, inspect all 64 signed contributions and their logit-sum residual, and keep the attack label separate. An algebraic tanh specialization is checked against independent 128-node Gauss-Legendre quadrature on 40 reference vectors, with maximum coordinate difference 3.56e-15 and maximum sum residual 1.07e-14 across all 7,188 official model/reference/row cases — finite numerical checks, not exact proofs. Both references are explicit — all-zero block counts and the frozen mean of only the 3,062 fitting rows, never validation or test rows — and the explained quantity is a selected class logit, not a softmax probability; neither reference is presumed neutral, realistic or causally meaningful. The v0.4 inspector runs manual JavaScript inference and input gradients with no upload or prediction service: paint with mouse, touch or keyboard, inspect all ten scores and compute clipped FGSM, with editing clearing the attack label, and a real browser checks 320/390/1280px layout, keyboard painting, touch dragging, both models, attack bounds, clear and restore. The v0.3 extension solves all 1,797 clipped adversarial-radius infima exactly — 99 clean errors, 796 positive attained thresholds and 902 positive unattained infima, with lowest-index ties deciding whether the boundary itself is an error and all 7,188 old box decisions agreeing; the first attained example is row 6 (true 6, competitor 4) and the first unattained is row 0 (true 0, competitor 9). The v0.2 extension adds exact real affine-box correctness for the fixed linear model — 1,698 / 1,495 / 1,028 / 23 of 1,797 test rows at radii 0 / 1/20 / 1/10 / 1/5 — with attained witnesses exposing 14 and 10 saved PGD survivors at the latter two radii, and all 64,692 margins and 7,188 witness decisions retained. The test counts grow with the versions: 30 tests in v0.1 including 201 independent gradient probes, full refit without test-loader access and saved-report reproduction; 45 in v0.2 including 144 all-vertex certificates and 160 independent Fraction row references; 59 in v0.3 including two independent 144-case vertex references, 160 Fraction row references, 100 official-boundary checks and three full-report figures; 64 Python tests and four Node/real-Chrome tests in v0.4 with 52 independent NumPy reference cases; 78 Python tests and six Node/real-Chrome tests in v0.5 with 106 attribution reference vectors plus the existing 52 inference/gradient cases, four reproducible audit figures and ten-module/ten-CLI wheel checks. The protocol is fixed and disclosed: UCI Optical Recognition of Handwritten Digits, not MNIST — 64 block counts in 0..16, normalized by 16 — with the original 3,823-row training file hash-partitioned per class into 3,062 fitting and 761 validation rows, the 1,797 official test rows kept separate, both models trained 80 epochs by seeded shuffled minibatch SGD with momentum and selected by minimizing validation NLL (both select epoch 80), and test examples parsed only after model selection. The scope is one historical benchmark and a fixed protocol, not superiority or SOTA claims; 1NN getting 1,678 correct on the MLP's epsilon-0.1 PGD images is transfer, not an adaptive attack on 1NN, and surviving these attacks is not proof of robustness. Original data, models and reports are preserved with CC BY 4.0 attribution.

- **ScanLab** - Exact affine prefix scans and input-gated state-space derivatives, built from scratch in pure Python — it keeps algorithmic span separate from real arithmetic work and from floating behavior, on supplied tiny affine tables where every state, gradient and count is an exact rational, not a claim about speed or trained-model quality. Diagonal affine pairs compose in temporal order; a reduction/reconstruction inclusive scan retains every operand, interval, pair and dependency depth. A fixed diagonal LTI recurrence equals direct causal convolution, with the nonzero-initial-state boundary term and skip connection explicit, and a separate sigmoid input-gated recurrence exposes every forward state and hand-derived parameter/input/initial-state adjoint, with streaming carrying state across chunk boundaries without changing causal outputs. The failure controls are the point: a floating cancellation control writes [1e16,1,-1e16,1] with decay 1, where the sequential final state is 1, the scan is 0 and the exact rational result is 2; and an input-gate control gives final f(2x)-2f(x) about 0.20243342, so one fixed linear convolution cannot represent that model on all inputs — neither failing control is turned into a tolerance-based success. The exact 16-token, two-state fixture uses 26 pair compositions: unit vector composition/apply span is 7 versus recurrence span 16, while scalar multiplications rise from 32 to 136 and additions from 32 to 84 — actual DAG counts on supplied pairs, not runtime, GPU/FLOPs or fraction bit complexity, and both paths execute serial Python. The v0.3 extension couples dense states: full rational transition matrices mix state coordinates, matrix-affine composition preserves temporal multiplication order, and reverse propagation uses the next transposed matrix, exposing every state JVP, full matrix/write/initial VJP, outer-product gradient and primal/tangent/reverse DAG. The exact transposition identity is -440563/15120 on both sides; omitting only the transpose in reverse propagation gives gap 7831/2520; reversing only matrix-product order, with correct write composition, changes final state [9/2,187/36] to [-14/9,2/3] — and both mistakes become invisible under diagonal restrictions, which are tested separately, while singular reset steps work without inverses. Dense composition costs cubic work in state width: the six-token, two-state fixture uses seven matrix-matrix products and thirteen matrix-vector products, 108 multiplications/80 additions versus sequential 24/24, with unit-pair span 4 versus 6 treating whole matrix operations as units, not hardware latency; Fifty-one instrumented rational cases count actual arithmetic calls, excluding tangent construction, gradient products, loss reductions and bit complexity, so they are not end-to-end derivative costs. The v0.2 extension records forward/tangent/reverse/loss-reverse/HVP-reverse scan DAGs with every parameter VJP and every state JVP, and hand-written forward-over-reverse rules compute squared-loss gradient and HVP without dense matrices: the exact transposition identity is -817/120 on both sides, a wrong reverse-index control using the current rather than next decay gives 139/360 with gap -259/36, fixture loss is 2473/288, first directional derivative 83689/17280 and second 20591177/414720, and an independent bivariate polynomial expansion checks 432 parameter coordinates across 36 models, including each VJP, gradient and HVP coordinate, with another 24 directional models, 24 Hessian symmetry checks and 51 independent future-decay product models. The v0.3 suite has 63 tests, preserving all 46 earlier tests and saved artifacts and adding 96 independent homogeneous-product shapes, full DAGs through length 32, 216 associativity cases, 672 full-polynomial VJP parameter coordinates across 36 models, 24 full-direction polynomial models and 27 diagonal restrictions, with three reproducible full-witness SVGs and six-command/nine-module installed wheel parity; the v0.2 increment had 46 tests including the original 30, with a new 68-cell figure embedding all five complete DAGs, the original 114-cell figure preserved, and five CI jobs including Python 3.10-3.13 and all four CLI commands from an installed eight-module wheel outside checkout; the original 30 tests include 729 scalar composition cases, 264 length/width cases against an independent product expansion, 120 fixed LTI models, 123 independent gradient probes, all 64 streaming partitions, full DAG/count checks and strict invalid inputs, with the 16 displayed gradient probes having maximum central-difference error about 8.419e-12. The scope is supplied tiny affine tables only — width 1..8 and length 0..128 in v0.1, width 1..4 and length 0..32 on the new dense path — with rational algebra and float sigmoid arithmetic as separate paths, finite differences validating fixtures rather than all derivatives, and no dense HVP, general AD engine, symbolic compiler, new scan algorithm, learned-task quality, general long-context memory, GPU speed, FFT, HiPPO or Mamba/S4 claim; seven prior sources are attributed in v0.1 and six sources in each of the v0.2 and v0.3 derivations, with the full equations, counters and independent tests in the source NOTES.md, ADJOINT.md and DENSE.md.
  - Case study: [scanlab.md](scanlab.md)
  - Repo: [midya8/scanlab](https://github.com/midya8/scanlab)

- **Correlab** - Exact-rational audits of supplied finite general-sum joint play, built from scratch in pure Python — it makes the difference between a joint law with no profitable fixed-action deviation and a joint law that still tells a player to replace their recommended action inspectable, on supplied small games where every gain, contribution and attaining deviation is an exact rational, not a claim about real-agent strength. A single regret number can hide that distinction, the probability of the signal, or the effect of removing correlation, so Correlab separates coarse fixed-action deviations, weighted one-pair internal deviations, unrestricted recommendation-dependent swap maps and conditional incentives, and it does not search for an equilibrium or train an agent. Every deviation retains all opponent-action probabilities, payoff differences and exact contributions; each fixed deviation retains recommendation-wise gains, and full-swap optimization separates by recommendation — choose an action maximizing each row of weighted gains, then add the maxima — with the report retaining every optimal choice, the number of optimal maps, the lexicographically first attaining map and all its joint-cell contributions. Conditional gains divide by the recommendation's positive probability and zero-probability recommendations return null, each player's gains stay separate, and the report also retains marginals, their product, exact product equality, total variation and an independently audited product control — only a product law that is CCE is labeled a Nash product. The controls that expose misleading summaries are the point: Roth's explicitly attributed lecture-8 CCE-not-CE game has uniform diagonal play with expected payoff 3/10, best raw fixed gain -3/10 and clipped coarse epsilon zero, yet recommendation C permits conditional gain 11/10 and weighted and full-swap gain 11/30; a developed coordination law pays 3/2 per player and is CE while its product-marginals control pays 3/4 with swap regret 1/4, so identical marginals do not imply identical joint laws or incentives; a rare recommendation has mass 1/1000 and weighted gain 1/1000 while its conditional incentive is one; and three equally likely recommendations with row payoffs [0,0,1] have largest one-pair gain 1/3 but full-swap gain 2/3, with an attaining map sending all recommendations to action 2. Realized joint actions produce exact prefix count laws, and independent sums of actual, fixed-action counterfactual and attaining-swap counterfactual payoffs certify that total regret equals prefix length times the corresponding empirical-law gain; final empirical reports are order-invariant while prefixes and last-round laws need not be, and the developed six-round history finishes with an empirical CCE while the final action pair has coarse epsilon 11/10 — a finite arithmetic identity and counterexample, not an implemented learner or no-regret guarantee. The v0.2 extension asks what the player observes: a trusted executor retains the hidden baseline action, the player sees only a supplied private signal and may pass or override with the opponent left at baseline — an explicit unilateral decision model, not CE of a changed game. Every signal/action retains exact joint-cell contributions, conditional gains, zero-mass nulls, all optimal choices and an attaining policy; identity signals recover full-swap gain and identical-row channels recover clipped coarse gain, and a supplied post-garbling retains both audits plus a lifted optimal coarse policy with exact nonnegative per-signal slacks summing to information loss. All deterministic own-action partitions through four actions are audited independently for each player with every strict refinement relation — the Bell counts are 1, 2, 5, 15 — and a developed cyclic control gives row gains 0, 4/3, 5/3, 1, 2 for partitions 000, 001, 010, 011, 012, showing three two-signal partitions can differ, while the attributed Roth control yields 0, 11/120, 11/60, 11/30 as C's reveal probability rises 0, 1/4, 1/2, 1. The v0.2 suite has 50 tests, including independent enumeration for 8,505 channel audits, 28 larger/rectangular four-signal controls up to 625 policies per player, 243 supplied post-garbling lift certificates and all 16 signal-alphabet size pairs checked by direct lift simulation; three new SVG tests bind 25 numeric cells, complete report metadata and hidden lift-term tampering, all seven complete CLI outputs match an installed seven-module wheel outside checkout, the original three CLI outputs and old SVG are unchanged, and six additional source notes distinguish single-agent value from strategic welfare. The original v0.1 verification has 33 tests covering all 81 ternary 2x2 row tables against all 35 quarter-grid joint laws with a separately varied column table — 2,835 game/law cases checked by exhaustive action-map enumeration — plus 28 rectangular/larger controls enumerating up to 256 maps per player, all 256 length-four 2x2 histories at three prefixes, and three SVG tests binding all 36 numeric cells, the full compressed report, hidden-term tampering and exact regeneration, with the six-module installed wheel matching all three full CLI outputs outside checkout and CI on Python 3.10-3.13 plus a package-parity job. Two reproducible SVGs. The scope is supplied exact payoff tables and joint laws only — one to four actions per side, at most 1,000 realized rounds and 100 explicit checkpoints — with no simultaneous override outcome, global stochastic-channel optimization, universal Blackwell-order search, privacy, learning or agent safety claim, and no scalable solver, adaptive algorithm, causal experiment, hidden-information/extensive-form game, real-agent benchmark or safety certification; epsilon values are payoff incentives, not distance to an equilibrium set or statistical confidence. The source DESIGN.md provides proofs, the complete schema and six consulted sources — Blum-Mansour, Bonn, Roth, Barakat, Charles University and Hart-Mas-Colell — crediting the adapted Roth fixture and recording cost/payoff orientation differences plus source-text typos rather than silently inheriting them.
  - Case study: [correlab.md](correlab.md)

- **GameLab** - Exact-rational zero-sum game audits with complete optimal faces, minimax witnesses and deterministic perturbation certificates, built from scratch in pure Python — it makes the difference between a unique game value and a unique optimal strategy inspectable, on supplied small zero-sum tables where every vertex, gap and bound is an exact rational, not a claim about real-agent strength. GameLab enumerates every active basis of an exact-rational security LP — at most 70 per player for a 4x4 table — retaining rank, determinant, pivots, all slacks and violations for every singular, infeasible and feasible attempt, then merges duplicate bases so all optimal vertices describe the full optimal face; the row player maximizes payoff while the column player minimizes it by solving the negative-transpose game, whose internal value has the opposite sign. A deterministic equal average of distinct optimal vertices supplies one equilibrium with pure-action payoff, best-reply, probability, primal/dual and complementary-slackness witnesses — not a uniquely preferred or maximum-entropy policy — and coordinate extrema include attaining vertices whose ranges are not independent choices. A separate strategy audit returns both unilateral gains and both security losses under the sum, not half-sum, exploitability convention. The failure worth seeing is the duplicate-action continuum: rock-paper-scissors has value zero and one-third mixed policies, yet both fixed action-zero policies also yield payoff zero while their unilateral gains sum to two, so a zero observed payoff is not an equilibrium certificate; in a 4x2 game with duplicate rows the optimal row vertices [0,1/2,1/2,0] and [1/2,0,1/2,0] make every convex combination optimal, the fourth action is dominated, and the representative [1/4,1/4,1/2,0] preserves the face rather than pretending it is unique; a constant 2x3 table has every policy optimal, and a separate nonzero game independently checks value 3/2 with row policy [1/4,3/4]. Strategies can be discontinuous even when values are stable: a two-row/one-column payoff flip of 10^-12 changes the unique optimal row from [0,1] to [1,0] with unchanged value, and a separate near-bound control approaches the two-epsilon transfer-gap bound — the payoff-change audit retains both complete solutions, entry differences, every maximum-difference location and exact bound slacks, so with epsilon the maximum entry change the value changes by at most epsilon and the old equilibrium pair has new gap at most two epsilon. The shared-table interval extension computes the exact worst joint deviation gap over a supplied full Cartesian entrywise interval box for one fixed policy pair facing one unknown payoff table, not two independently selected adverse tables: for every pure reply pair it constructs the signed coefficient of every entry before maximizing, retains endpoint choices and contributions, and returns an attaining matrix with a direct best-reply audit, while also reporting each player’s separately maximized unilateral gain and the conservatism of their sum. In the developed shared-entry control only A[0,0] varies in [0,2], all other entries are one and both policies choose action zero; the row worst deviation requires entry zero and the column worst requires entry two, so the exact shared worst gap is one while their incompatible separate maxima sum to two, the game value stays one throughout, and the separate robust-security pair is an equilibrium for every allowed table. For a full 2x2 unit box with uniform fixed policies the shared worst gap is 1/2 versus a separate worst-regret sum of one, and for a one-action game with sole payoff [-50,50] the value-range width is 100 but every policy pair’s gap is zero — payoff uncertainty is not the same as incentive to deviate. Complete endpoint game solves certify the attainable game-value extrema v(L) and v(U); the lower-game row policy and upper-game column policy separately optimize worst security and guarantee pair gap at most v(U)-v(L), with a signed audit that can be tighter, though the construction does not claim to minimize worst joint gap over policies. The 53 tests cover all 81 ternary 2x2 matrices against an independent one-dimensional envelope oracle, independent permutation determinants and every basis residual on four controls, optimal-vertex cross-pairs and convex mixtures, coordinate attaining witnesses, all 81 grid strategy pairs, affine/permutation/negative-transpose invariances, perturbation cases, 4x4 capacity and strict input failures — plus the extension’s all 16 zero/one-width boxes on a nonconstant 2x2 base, each at nine fixed policy pairs, against independent exhaustive endpoint enumeration, every candidate support contribution and attainment, corner game values and robust security, shared-entry and singleton cancellation, affine, zero-width, rectangular and 4x4 controls, strict schemas and no mutation; SVG checks bind 23 cells, four range segments and eight endpoints, then 29 numbers in the interval figure, with complete compressed report metadata, hidden-basis and hidden-coefficient tampering and exact regeneration. Two reproducible SVGs, six-module wheel parity reproducing all six complete CLI JSON outputs outside checkout with the original 37 tests and four CLI outputs unchanged, and Python 3.10-3.13 CI plus a package-parity job. The scope is small supplied tables only — 1..4 actions per side, exact bounded inputs, no runtime dependency or network call — with no general-sum, hidden-information, extensive-form, correlated-equilibrium, learned-opponent, payoff-estimation, scalable-solver or new-algorithm claim, and developed numerical fixtures are not held-out tournaments or evidence of real-agent strength. The project implements established minimax, LP duality and polytope geometry, with six consulted sources, the full proof, schemas and orientation differences in the source DESIGN.md and box support-function proofs in INTERVALS.md, crediting Li-Ding-Floudas, Gorissen-den Hertog and Bertsimas-den Hertog-Pauphilet.
  - Case study: [gamelab.md](gamelab.md)
  - Repo: [midya8/gamelab](https://github.com/midya8/gamelab)

- **BetLab** - Exact sequential evidence for bounded paired model scores, built from scratch in pure Python — it makes the difference between a valid anytime test and a ruined test quietly given a free restart inspectable, on disclosed small fixtures where every wealth update, crossing probability and confidence bracket is an exact rational, not a claim about a deployed monitor. For differences D_t in [-1,1] under the one-sided conditional null E[D_t | past] <= 0, any past-measurable bet b_t in [0,1] multiplies wealth by 1+b_t*D_t, and a fixed convex mixture of such wealth processes stays one, so Ville's inequality bounds the probability of ever reaching 1/alpha by alpha at any stopping time within the fixed test. Two valid constructions are exposed separately: a fixed mixture over bets [0,1/4,1/2,3/4,1] with equal positive weights by default, and a past-only heuristic clip(sum_previous_D / (1 + sum_previous_D_squared), 0, 1) that excludes the current observation — a separate test, not an extra component chosen after seeing results. The failure worth seeing is the invalid free restart: on the eight-round fair-coin null tree (255 internal histories, 256 equiprobable leaves) at alpha 1/4, the fixed mixture crosses threshold 4 with probability 5/64 and the past-only strategy 9/128, both with expected final and stopped wealth 1, while injecting a fresh unit before the next round whenever wealth hits zero crosses with probability 201/256, expected final wealth 9/2 and stopped wealth 423/128 — the chance of at least one run of two positive signs in eight fair flips — and the node audit exposes negative conditional-expectation slack after ruin, so it is a counterexample to free refills, not to optional stopping. The six-round negative-drift tree (positive-sign probability 1/3, conditional mean -1/3) crosses with probability 1/81 for both valid constructions and 281/729 for the invalid restart. A reversal sequence of eight +1 then twelve -1 first crosses threshold 4 at round 4 for both valid processes, then ends below 4; a separate 32-round paired fixture with baseline 1/2 and candidate 3/4 crosses threshold 20 at round 19 for the mixture and 17 for the past-only heuristic. The start-allocation extension replaces free refills with a prespecified alternative: allocate one initial unit across deterministic start times before observing the stream — at most 16 starts in 1..256, positive weights summing exactly to one, ruined components staying zero and unstarted components contributing their reserved weight — and the complete statistic is a fixed convex mixture, not the largest start-specific result. On the delayed-signal control (eight negative, eight positive, eight negative differences), bet one with starts 1, 9, 17 and equal weights first crosses threshold 4 at round 12, has wealth 257/3 including reserve 1/3 at round 16, and ends at zero, while the one-start control is permanently ruined at round one. The fair eight-round null audit with starts 1, 3, 5, 9, each weight 1/4, keeps the round-nine budget reserved at every leaf and crosses with probability 5/32 at alpha 1/4 with final and stopped expectations both 1, while the invalid free-refill control stays 201/256; the six-round negative-drift law gives 17/729 with expected final wealth 1261/2916 and expected stopped wealth 683/1458. The confidence-sequence inversion extension adds a deliberately conservative bounded-mean bet family with factors 1+b*(D-m)/2 for each candidate mean m in [-1,1]; monotone wealth makes the accepted mean set an interval, and exact rational bisection retains component-level crossing/noncrossing witnesses and every midpoint comparison, with running intersections retaining earlier exclusions after reversal. With bet one and alpha 1/4, 24 repeated differences of 1/2 give boundary bracket [12487/32768,1561/4096] and conservative closed interval [12487/32768,1]; eight +1 then twelve -1 retain the earlier interval [1273/2048,1] even while the current-prefix inversion weakens; sixteen bisections bound running-boundary uncertainty by 1/32768. An eight-round sign law with positive probability 3/4 and constant conditional mean 1/2 enumerates all 256 paths: ever-exclusion and true-mean crossing probabilities are both 2187/16384, below alpha 1/4, with every conditional mixture slack zero and final/stopped expectations one. The 67 tests build on the original 34 — independent exhaustive product references for all 243 length-five ternary sequences, a separate 64-path crossing/expectation reference, exact fair and negative-drift trees, predictable-prefix checks, all 256 observations and 16 mixture components, zero wealth, equality at threshold, running p-bound monotonicity, a marginal-zero but conditionally invalid law, variable-depth/zero-difference controls, internal rational differences larger than input component caps, alias/cycle/scope/input guards, and all four CLI outputs and error paths — with the start-allocation extension adding direct suffix-product references, all 256 fair paths, every conditional expectation and full 256-observation/16-start/16-bet capacity, and the confidence extension adding exhaustive independent products/brackets/trials on 81 ternary streams, exact endpoint/midpoint ties, refinement, reversal, prefix-only behavior, all 256 coverage paths and full capacity; SVG checks cover all 63 plotted coordinates, all nine null-table values, 92 new-figure coordinates and seven cells, full compressed report metadata, hidden-witness tampering and byte-exact regeneration. Three reproducible SVGs, seven-module wheel parity reproducing all ten complete CLI JSON outputs outside checkout, and Python 3.10-3.13 CI. The scope is small supplied laws and developed fixtures only — at most 64 observations, 16 fixed bets, 1..32 bisections and eight-round trees — with no unbounded-score method, learned optimal bet, automatic stream selection, multiple-test correction, drifting-mean or causal-effect estimate, or production monitoring service; a crossing is not a posterior probability, an effect-size estimate or proof that a particular model is generally better, and non-crossing is not evidence of equivalence. The betting mathematics follows Waudby-Smith and Ramdas with supporting e-process references; this is an original small implementation, not a new inference theorem.

- **PrivacyLab** - Exact finite-channel differential privacy with rational hockey-stick events, built from scratch in pure Python — it makes the difference between a marginally private-looking release and a jointly private one inspectable, on disclosed small fixtures where every delta, factor and witness is an exact rational, not a claim about a deployed mechanism. PrivacyLab audits supplied finite probability tables against explicit neighboring-secret edges, computing the smallest delta at an exact multiplicative factor t=exp(epsilon) with every directed-pair event witness retained; factor 3 means epsilon ln(3), not epsilon 3. The failure worth seeing is the shared-coin control: with Z zero with probability 3/4, releases A=secret XOR Z and B=Z have pure factors 3 and 1 alone, but A XOR B recovers the secret exactly, so the joint release has delta 1 for every finite factor — marginals do not compose when randomness is shared. The developed table keeps every factor and delta exact: randomized response at truthful probability 3/4 has factor 3 with deltas 1/2, 0, 0 at factors 1, 3, 9; two independent releases factor 9 with 1/2, 3/8, 0; a vote with a fresh fair tie-break factor 3 with 1/2, 0, 0; parity only factor 1 with 0, 0, 0; the shared-coin joint infinite with 1, 1, 1. The adaptive extension unfolds full history-conditioned mechanism trees: fixed round factors multiply to T, a substochastic clipping kernel bounds the actual joint delta by a telescoping leaf-mass deficit, and the fair-branch control has actual/clipped delta 1/2 against uniform/additive bounds of 1, while a revealing but impossible history kills zero mass and the shared-coin joint is reproduced exactly by its true conditional laws. The factor-frontier extension computes the complete worst-neighbor delta profile for every finite factor, certifying each interval against every overlapping local piece: on the developed three-secret/four-output control with rows [1/16,5/16,1/8,1/2], [5/16,3/8,1/16,1/4], [1/8,5/16,3/16,3/8] and edges 0-1, 1-2, the profile is 5/8-5t/16 from 1 to 5/4, 5/16-t/16 from 5/4 to 5, and zero after 5, with the maximizing direction switching at 5/4 — absent from the likelihood-ratio grid {1,6/5,3/2,2,5/2,3,5} — where both directions tie at delta 15/64. Exact inverse delta budgets retain minimality witnesses or singular-support obstructions: delta 1/4 needs factor 6/5, 15/64 needs 5/4, 0 needs 5, a singular plateau of 1/2 makes target 1/4 unattainable at any finite factor, and a full-support control needs the finite factor 500000000000000000000000000000. The 63 tests cover the original 29 — 24 seeded channels against exhaustive all-16-event searches per directed pair at four factors, a 10^-100 singular support leak kept exact, 36 SVG cells — plus 14 adaptive behavior and 3 SVG checks (24 seeded three-round/three-secret trees, exhaustive all-256-event searches per directed pair, six rounds, 64 leaves, eight secrets, all 28 edges) and 14 frontier behavior and 3 SVG checks (32 seeded three-secret/four-output channels, exhaustive all-16-event references, inverse checks at four targets, ten new query cells). Three reproducible SVGs, seven-module wheel parity reproducing all six complete CLI JSON outputs outside checkout, and Python 3.10-3.13 CI. The scope is small supplied tables only — at most 8 secrets, 64 outputs and 28 undirected edges in the point audit, four secrets and eight outputs in the frontier, six rounds in the adaptive trees — with no trained-model certification, secure sampler, real-data adjacency proof, DP-SGD accountant, advanced composition or production privacy guarantee. Established DP and hockey-stick arithmetic follows Dwork and Roth with modern accounting notes; this is an original small implementation, not a new privacy theorem or production accountant.

- **SparseLab** - LASSO optimization audits with exact primal/dual/KKT witnesses, built from scratch in pure Python — it makes the difference between a sparse-looking coefficient vector and an identified one inspectable, on disclosed small fixtures where every gap, KKT residual and branch is an exact rational or converged numerical reference, not a claim about held-out accuracy. Dependency-free cyclic coordinate descent minimizes `||y-X beta||^2/(2N) + lambda*||beta||_1` with every column penalized and no hidden intercept, standardization, centering or ridge term; every sweep reports primal loss/penalty, an interior scaled-residual feasible dual witness, all correlations, a stable gap decomposition, direct primal-minus-dual difference, all KKT residuals and support, and both absolute gap and maximum KKT residual must reach tolerance or the run is an explicit nonconvergence result. The ambiguity controls are the point: on duplicate columns at lambda=1/4 the forward fit `[7/4,0,-1/2]`, reverse fit `[0,7/4,-1/2]` and midpoint `[7/8,7/8,-1/2]` all reach objective `5/8` with identical predictions and zero KKT residual, so unique predictions do not identify coefficients or a causal selected feature; on correlated columns at lambda=1/8 one sweep leaves gap `0.330882353` and max KKT `0.176470588`, 18 sweeps reach objective `19/68`, and the exact rational active/sign enumerator independently agrees with `[0,33/17]`. The Gap Safe extension audits any supplied iterate with exact rational arithmetic over the stored binary64 inputs: a positive exact margin `lambda-|X_j^T u|` with `||X_j||^2*R_squared < margin^2` proves coefficient j zero in every optimizer — a near iterate with current beta_2=1/32 has gap `5/1024` and certifies feature 2 (ball term `5/512` < squared margin `25/1024`, slack `15/1024`), a loose iterate with a zero column has gap `73/32` and certifies it anyway, while an exact boundary zero has radius=margin=0 and stays uncertified, a duplicate-column optimum screens neither copy, and an equality control at ball=squared margin=1/4 stays uncertified — a sufficient certificate, not a complete support detector. The affine-branch extension verifies the drop/re-entry phenomenon with exact KKT branches instead of a dense grid of approximate fits: on the developed centered four-row fixture with columns a and 2a+b, response 2a-b, Gram `[[1,2],[2,5]]` and cross `[2,3]`, five supplied branches cover `[3,infinity)`, `[4/3,3]`, `[1,4/3]`, `[1/3,1]` and `[0,1/3]` — feature 1 enters positive at 3, feature 0 enters at 4/3, feature 1 leaves at 1 and re-enters negative at 1/3, adjacent knots agree exactly in coefficients, predictions, objectives and dual vectors, and the zero-penalty endpoint is `[4,-1]`; selected signs are weak, so boundary zeros are allowed and an infeasible pattern returns an explicit noncertificate rather than a fabricated answer. The 62 tests cover the original 28 — independent scalar/orthogonal formulas, ten seeded exact-rational comparisons, full five-column enumeration, duplicate convex mixtures, weak duality, all primal/dual equations, monotone sweep histories, warm/cold agreement, explicit path failure propagation, symmetries, row replication, scale/penalty equivalence, zero columns, strict malformed JSON, nonmutation and RNG isolation, with three SVG checks over all 18 coefficient plot points, 23 table cells and byte-exact regeneration — plus 14 behavior and 3 SVG checks for the Gap Safe extension (24 seeded designs against independent active/sign-oracle witnesses, exact dual-optimum containment, 27 SVG cells) and 14 behavior and 3 SVG checks for the branch extension (12 seeded five-row/three-feature designs exercising all 27 sign patterns, 20 figure cells, both path geometries with all 10 vertices, five knots, hidden singular-Gram tampering). Three reproducible SVGs, seven-module wheel parity reproducing all six complete CLI JSON outputs outside checkout with all three SVGs regenerating byte-exactly, and Python 3.10-3.13 CI. The scope is small dense fits only — at most 128 rows and 12 columns, exact oracle at most 32 rows and 5 columns, positive lambda — with floating-point dual/KKT checks that are not outward-rounded certificates and no coefficient error bounds in rank-deficient problems; no held-out accuracy, cross-validation, causal feature importance or post-selection inference. Standard coordinate descent and soft thresholding follow Friedman, Hastie and Tibshirani, KKT and dual geometry follow Tibshirani's coordinate-descent notes, Gap Safe rules follow Fercoq, Gramfort and Salmon, and piecewise-linear LASSO paths follow Efron, Hastie, Johnstone and Tibshirani with Rosset and Zhu — this is an original small implementation with developed controls, not a new algorithm, a general LARS solver, a complete support-recovery method or a speed benchmark.

- **InfluenceLab** - Convex logistic influence functions with converged Newton fits and full Hessian/solve witnesses, built from scratch in pure Python — it makes the gap between a local weight derivative and an actual row deletion inspectable, on a disclosed seven-row fixture where every derivative, refit and deletion path is a converged numerical reference, not symbolic arithmetic. InfluenceLab fits small fully regularized binary logistic models with Newton steps, unjittered Cholesky solves, Armijo backtracking and an explicit numerical convergence requirement, reporting the full Hessian, Cholesky factors, parameter directions, query-loss derivatives, adjoint agreement, solve residuals and all refit histories; there is no implicit intercept, and a supplied constant column is regularized too. Seven synthetic rows, query (1,2) with label 1, ridge 0.1 and original normalizer 7 give theta approximately (-0.275488,0.220249) and query loss 0.614042. For row 5 the linear fixed-N deletion prediction is +0.203204 while the actual fixed-N numerical refit is +0.305655, a linear-minus-refit error of -0.102451; the retained-row mean with the same ridge gives +0.317231 and a damped inverse with delta 0.5 gives +0.119499, so neither shortcut equals the refit — fixed-N deletion equals retained-mean deletion only with ridge multiplied by N/(N-1), not with unchanged ridge, and adding 0.5 to the inverse Hessian is not refitting a ridge-0.6 model. All 15 base/deletion numerical fits have max absolute gradient at most 8.197e-11 and undamped influence solves have max residual 5.551e-17; residuals measure numerical equations, not statistical uncertainty or deletion accuracy. The `trace_deletion` extension continuously lowers selected weights from 1 to 0 at 17 grid nodes, refitting from zero at each node and checking integrated local derivatives against actual endpoint loss across 51 fits in three groups: row 5 has Simpson16 delta +0.305655349 against an endpoint +0.305655, residual +1.381e-7; rows 5 and 6 jointly have Simpson16 -0.357502140 against an endpoint -0.357507, residual +4.825e-6, while their separate finite-deletion changes sum to only -0.142898, so group deletion is not a sum of separate refits even though the group derivative at the base point is the sum of individual derivatives; deleting all seven rows reverses the frozen first-order sign, -0.004253 against an actual +0.079105, with Simpson16 +0.079849431 leaving a visible residual of +7.442e-4 from the sharp derivative tail, and the all-row endpoint has theta zero and query loss log(2). A small successive quadrature difference is not an error certificate, and monotone error reduction is not promised for arbitrary inputs. The 43 tests cover the original 26 — scalar fit against a separate monotone bisection optimizer, primitive and full-objective finite differences, every parameter weight derivative against perturbed converged refits on the fixture and three seeded systems up to six parameters, independent 2D inverse and adjoint solves, curvature sum-of-squares, strong-convexity error diagnostics, actual deletion and normalization equivalence, local quadratic remainder behavior, orthogonal-coordinate/permutation/label-complement checks, duplicate/zero features, explicit nonconvergence and safeguarded backtracking, strict JSON and stable extreme logits, with three of them inspecting every chart cell/position and the complete compressed JSON metadata including hidden refit-Hessian tampering — plus 14 new behavior tests for the path extension (quadrature against independent polynomial integrals, interior parameter/query derivatives against perturbed refits, all-node equations and convergence, local sum identities, direct grouped endpoints, sign reversal/nonadditivity, grid fit reuse, symmetry and strict API/CLI) and three SVG tests checking summary/convergence cells, all 51 polyline points, full metadata and hidden interior-Hessian tampering. Two reproducible SVGs, five-module wheel parity reproducing all four complete JSON reports outside checkout with both SVGs regenerating byte-exactly, and Python 3.10-3.13 CI. The scope is converged floating-point references on small positive-ridge convex logistic models: no causal attribution, bad-label detection guarantee, privacy deletion, machine-unlearning guarantee, neural-network scalability or confidence interval, and no paid APIs, runtime dependencies or private datasets. Standard influence-function mathematics is credited to Koh and Liang (2017) Section 2.1 for the inverse-Hessian derivative — this API uses unit sample weights inside the original-N average, hence the explicit 1/N — with Basu, Pope and Feizi noted for deep-model fragility this project does not reproduce; the fixture was developed during implementation, and this is an original small implementation, not a new explanation algorithm.
