- **FisherLab** - Exact-rational natural-policy-gradient geometry, built from scratch in pure Python with no runtime dependencies — it makes the qualifiers on "parameterization invariant" executable, on a disclosed three-action fixture where every Fisher matrix, nullspace and finite-step control is computed exactly. Three actions have probabilities (1/2,1/3,1/6) and rewards (0,1,3), so expected reward is 5/6 and reward variance is 41/36; four full-tangent charts — baseline rows (1,0),(0,1),(0,0), shear rows (1,2),(0,1),(0,0), full logits, and a redundant four-parameter chart — give exactly the same natural probability tangent (-5/12,1/18,13/36), with natural L1 gap 0 in every chart, while diagonal approximations miss by 5/9, 14/15, 31/60 and 37/20 and coordinate damping G+(1/10)I misses by 7/17, 43/213, 63/278 and 31/216, so the gaps are L1 distances to the full-simplex natural tangent, not performance scores, and these particular differences demonstrate non-invariance rather than a universal ranking of approximation methods. A restricted one-parameter chart has rank 1 and gaps 4/9, 4/9 and 11/21, and its tangent (-5/12,5/18,5/36) is the exact Fisher-metric projection into its smaller reachable space, not a failed solver. The finite-step trap is separate: a binary model with p(action1)=sigmoid(z), rewards (0,1) and local coordinate z=theta^3 at theta=1 has natural directions dz=1 and dtheta=1/3, so the initial mapped logit tangent agrees exactly, but at step eta=1/2 Euler updates give logit 3/2 directly versus 343/216 in the curved chart, an exact difference of 19/216, with updated action-1 probabilities approximately 0.817574 versus 0.830329; and a step of 1 along the exact logit natural direction on the three-action fixture has actual KL(p_old || p_new)=0.725586 against a local quadratic prediction of 41/72=0.569444, with a linear reward prediction of 71/36=1.972222 against an actual 2.303238 — local quantities are not exact finite-step constraints or promises. The shared-context extension aggregates exact Fisher and reward-gradient systems across 1-6 fixed contexts sharing parameters: with context frequencies (3/4,1/4), equal binary action probabilities and rewards (0,1) and (0,-2), a shared parameter has natural direction 1/4, total reward derivative positive 1/64, but the second context's derivative is negative -1/8, and the full-conditional target has exact squared length 7/16 = 1/64 + 27/64, reachable plus metric-orthogonal residual, while independent context parameters reach both targets with zero residual as a larger model, not a training benchmark; replacing frequencies with uniform weights changes the objective, its natural direction -1/2 having derivative -1/32 on the original weighted objective, and a separate context-gauge control is frozen under conditional normalization but has spurious nonzero Fisher and gradient if naively modeled as a global joint softmax. Output carries the logit Fisher, probability Jacobian, pullback Fisher, per-action score rows, two independent-form Gram reconstructions, a positive weighted pairwise outer-product PSD certificate, gradient, full RREF augmented matrix, pivot and free columns, all nullspace directions, solve residual and induced null tangents, with per-direction parameter, logit and probability vectors, objective derivative, Fisher length, probability-space length and L1 gap; the exact RREF representative sets free variables to zero and is not a Moore-Penrose pseudoinverse or minimum-Euclidean-norm solution, and for full charts the natural objective derivative and both metric lengths equal reward variance, while restricted charts report rank and projection gap explicitly. Inputs are 2-8 strictly positive rational probabilities summing exactly to 1, aligned rational rewards, 1-12 coordinate columns and positive rational damping, with integers, Fractions and rational or decimal strings accepted, floats and booleans rejected, rational inputs capped at 128 numerator/denominator bits and strings at 100 characters, and the CLI requiring exactly version 1, probabilities, rewards, coordinates and damping, rejecting duplicate and unknown fields and files over 100KB, with invalid calls exiting 2 without a JSON result. The 45 tests cover the original 28 — 25 behavior tests including independently computed finite-difference reward and probability derivatives, every entry of a KL Hessian, exact closed forms, 16 seeded chart configurations up to 8 actions and 12 parameters, gauge, reward-shift and ordering invariance, frozen, restricted and overcomplete charts, complete singular-system residual and null witnesses, sum-of-squares quadratic certificates, finite-step controls, strict API and CLI errors, immutability and global RNG isolation, plus three SVG tests verifying all 60 table cells and positions, exact nonlinear logit controls, complete compressed report metadata, hidden witness tampering and byte regeneration — and 17 more for the context extension, 14 behavior plus three SVG, including independent weighted reward and KL finite differences, centered joint-score reconstruction, exact projection and null certificates, strict CLI and conditional-versus-joint controls. Two reproducible SVGs and dependency-free Python 3.10-3.13 CI running both CLI paths, SVG regeneration and installed-wheel comparisons outside the source checkout, with the demo asserting both agreement and counterexamples and the committed full report, strict input, proof and scope document and byte-reproducible SVG making the numbers inspectable. The scope is full-support categorical expected reward only: no MDP transitions, estimated occupancies, learned critics, sampled Fisher or gradient noise, policy training, neural networks, line search, trust-region enforcement or convergence and optimizer-superiority benchmark; boundary probabilities are rejected rather than silently regularized, damping is deliberately coordinate-identity damping rather than an invariantly transformed regularizer, and the input Jacobian alone does not specify any nonlinear finite update. Standard natural-gradient and Fisher ideas are credited to Kakade (2001) Section 2 and Martens (2020) Sections 5 and 9 on Fisher and pullback, Section 10 on damping and Section 12 on infinitesimal versus finite-step parameterization invariance — but this is an original small-model implementation, exact witnesses, controls and test suite, not a new algorithm or a reproduction of published training experiments.

- **LatentLab** - A trained Gaussian variational autoencoder built from scratch in scalar Python — it makes the gap between reconstructing well and generating well inspectable, on a disclosed synthetic fixture where every gradient, evidence estimate and failure control is computed exactly or audited. The 150-parameter model uses 12-unit tanh hidden layers in both encoder and decoder, a diagonal posterior with smooth bounded logvariance, and a Gaussian decoder likelihood with fixed standard deviation 0.2; reparameterized latent samples and analytic Gaussian KL feed a hand-derived backward pass through every weight, bias and logvariance path, with Adam bias correction, and a strict versioned JSON checkpoint supports detached export/import, reproducible inference and prior sampling. Both arms train on the same 128 synthetic eight-mode points from identical initialized weights and data/noise RNG streams for 800 minibatch Adam updates, with neither seed nor checkpoint selected on held-out outcomes, and another 128 points are used for evaluation; negative ELBO uses four held-fixed posterior-noise draws per point with analytic KL, and prior generation includes independent observation-likelihood noise, not only decoded means. The held-out table makes the point concrete: negative ELBO 13.5149 untrained, 8.0758 at beta 0, 1.6600 at beta 1; analytic posterior KL 0.3324, 9.3704, 2.2073; posterior-mean MSE per coordinate 0.44105, 0.00268, 0.00281; prior-sample sliced W2 squared 0.1138, 0.0676, 0.0241; and 83.59%, 71.09%, 51.56% of prior samples outside radius 0.3 of all mode centers — the no-KL arm has slightly lower deterministic reconstruction error yet worse prior-sample distance, beta 1 improves the measured prior samples but over half still lie outside the disclosed neighborhoods, and the observation noise 0.2 is wider than the fixture jitter 0.06, so the distance diagnostic also reflects likelihood-family mismatch; sliced transport uses 32 deterministic directions and radius 0.3 is a disclosed synthetic diagnostic, not calibrated support estimation. The importance extension keeps the VAE unchanged and adds a separate inference audit with complete importance witnesses — latent draws, joint/proposal log densities, normalized weights, log mean weight, mean log weight and ESS — returning the analytic-KL MC negative ELBO separately because it is not the same finite-sample quantity as negative mean log weight, and only expectation bounds carry the IWAE monotonic hierarchy, so one random prefix need not improve. For the trained beta 1 anchor x=(1,0), shared Gaussian-noise prefixes of size 1, 4, 16 and 64 give log-evidence estimates -2.0464, 0.7106, -0.2701 and -0.9271, visibly nonmonotone, while the true nonlinear evidence is not known and no finite random row is guaranteed to bound it. A separate linear-Gaussian model has exact log evidence -2.15379530672424, verified by four independent Simpson-integral reference settings, and with its exact posterior as proposal all weights are constant and the 64-draw estimate recovers that value. The two-atom failure control is stronger than a seed-picked bad run: joint masses (0.000001, 0.999999) against proposal masses (0.999, 0.001) with true evidence 1, so with probability (999/1000)^64 = 93.79749638258456% all 64 draws hit the common proposal atom, ESS is then 64, but the log estimate is -13.814510 rather than 0, an underestimate by factor 999,000 — full support is present and nearly all joint mass is simply unseen, so high ESS only measures observed weight balance, not missed mass. Finite binomial enumeration with exact rational event probabilities gives expected evidence exactly 1 for every K, while expected log estimates for K=1, 4, 16, 64 are about -13.793788, -13.737278, -13.529377 and -12.785808: they rise and stay below 0 while remaining far from the truth. The 43 tests cover the original 25 — independent central differences of every parameter in nine hidden-size/beta settings, a fixed-noise batch derivative, saturated logvariance derivatives, zero-model analytic loss, exact-prior KL, an independently calculated first Adam update, strict checkpoint validation, immutable data, no global RNG effects, deterministic training and CLI success/failure paths, with the unchanged zero-step model deliberately failing the improvement gate and a collapsed origin cloud failing the disclosed distribution diagnostic — plus 15 importance tests and three figure tests, including exhaustive binary IID sequences through K=8, independent normal-density products, extreme-log underflow reports, exact-posterior constants, expected ELBO/posterior-KL identities, nonmutating inference, strict CLI validation and the real neural prefix fixture; three visual tests check all 12 metric lines, all 1,536 scatter coordinates and common axes, byte reproduction, complete compressed-report metadata and tampering with an undisplayed checkpoint weight, and the new figure tests check all 48 numeric cells/positions, complete witness metadata and an undisplayed log-joint change. Two reproducible SVGs and a five-module v0.2 wheel that reproduces both demos outside checkout, with original demo output and original SVG byte-identical, dependency-free Python 3.10-3.13 CI that runs the actual training demo, SVG check and wheel-installed demo outside checkout, with installed and source runs compared as complete JSON objects. Standard variational autoencoding, reparameterization and analytic Gaussian KL are credited to Kingma & Welling, and standard importance/IWAE formulas to Burda, Grosse & Salakhutdinov — but this is an original scalar implementation, synthetic fixture, audit and test suite, not a new algorithm or a reproduction of their experiments, and it is inference auditing, not IWAE training, a confidence interval, a new estimator, an ESS accuracy certificate or exact neural evidence: only beta 1 is the ordinary negative-ELBO objective, the posterior is diagonal with bounded variance, output noise is fixed, and there is only one two-dimensional data fixture and default seed, with no posterior-collapse, disentanglement, image-generation or generalization claim.
