- **ErgodLab** - Exact stationary policy sensitivities, independently checked against directed spanning trees, built from scratch in pure Python — it makes the difference between a one-step quantity and a long-run quantity inspectable, on supplied finite models where every stationary distribution, derivative and variance can be computed exactly. The anchored policy is proportional to `weight(s,a)*exp(feature(s,a) dot theta)`, with fixed transitions and rewards and theta evaluated at zero; exact pivoted linear solves return stationary distributions and derivatives, and a structurally independent directed spanning-tree product/quotient rule returns the same values and every derivative. The occupancy fixture makes the point concrete: state rewards are 0 and 1 and independent of the action, the controlled transition is `[[1/2,1/2],[1/4,3/4]]`, the stationary distribution is `[1/3,2/3]`, the average reward is `2/3` and the stationary derivative is `[-1/18,1/18]` — freezing that distribution gives an immediate-reward gradient of zero, while the true average-reward gradient is `1/18`, so a policy change that only moves where the chain spends its time is invisible to a frozen-distribution calculation. Two separate symmetric chains with state rewards 0/1 have equal one-step stationary reward variance `1/4`, yet staying in the same state with probability `7/8` gives asymptotic reward-sum variance per step `7/4` while deterministic alternation gives zero: the alternating chain has period two, so its exact stationary block-sum variance is zero for even lengths and `1/4` for odd lengths, and the figure shows all lengths 1..16 with both exact limits on the same axis. A centered Poisson solve returns `h` satisfying `(I-P)h=r-rho` and `mu h=0`, and the average-reward gradient is checked in both forms, `d rho = (dmu) r + mu dr = mu dr + mu (dP) h`; for every positive state/action/next-state edge the report returns the centered martingale increment `D=R-rho+h(next)-h(state)` and its nonnegative variance contribution, whose stationary second moment is the asymptotic variance of centered reward sums per step, and exact conditional first/second-moment dynamic programs return every finite-block variance through the requested length without enumerating exponentially many paths. The controlled chain must be irreducible, and periodicity is computed and reported rather than silently ruled out. The discounted extension separates the true fixed-start objective gradient from a stationary proxy: the optional `audit_discounted` API and `discount` CLI compute exact centered resolvents, their parameter derivatives and every residual, distinguishing the stationary average-reward gradient proxy from the true gradient of `(1-beta) E_start[sum beta^t R_t]` with the starting state fixed, and returning discounted occupancy, proxy bias and the antisymmetric derivative matrix — curl, not a full Jacobian. On a separate two-coordinate fixture with controlled switch probabilities `1/2,1/4`, derivatives `1/8,1/16` and average-reward gradient `[1/18,-1/18]`, at beta `1/2` the proxy is `[1/21,-1/21]` while the true fixed-start objective is `2/7` with gradient `[5/98,-1/98]`, and the proxy's exact curl entry is `-1/147`, ruling out a local scalar potential — though zero curl alone is not a sufficient global-potential test. At beta `1` the audit solves the centered Poisson limit instead of inverting the singular discounted matrix, recovering the true average-reward gradient and zero curl, including periodic chains; beta input is at most eight distinct rational pairs through denominator 10,000, and no monotone-bias or sampled-estimator claim is made. 39 tests cover 25 analytic two-state transition settings, 16 exact policy-weight gradient settings, nine multi-state/two-coordinate determinant and finite-difference references, explicit path enumerations through four steps for all three fixtures, the alternating chain through length 32, period-three chains, IID action rewards, zero-asymptotic-variance telescoping edge rewards, feature/reward/state/action transformations, five-state complete-graph tree counts, reducible refusal, strict CLI/schema/file bounds, input/output/RNG isolation and every SVG point/limit geometry; ten of those tests check an exact 16-weight/four-beta proxy/curl grid, independent Cramer/determinant finite differences across three models, all seven starting-state choices and four betas, true fixed-start gradients, near-one/zero/one/periodic/IID cases, transformations, all residuals and strict CLI/isolation, and three new figure tests check every exact table cell and its position, full metadata and tamper rejection. Input is a versioned JSON model with 1..5 states, 1..4 actions and 1..3 shared coordinates, with rational probabilities and rewards bounded, transition rows summing exactly to one, and duplicate keys, booleans, floats and malformed shapes rejected; the independent tree reference checks at most 1,280 parent assignments. Two reproducible SVGs and a six-module v0.2 wheel that reproduces the complete discount demo outside checkout, with both original audit and demo outputs unchanged, dependency-free Python 3.10+. Stationary sensitivities, directed spanning-tree formulas, Poisson equations and martingale decompositions are standard — the repository cites Aldous and Fill Section 9.2, Glynn and Infanger's Poisson-equation framework, and Greensmith, Bartlett and Baxter's average-reward policy-gradient context — but this is not GPOMDP, a learned policy, a sampled gradient estimator, a confidence interval, a mixing-rate or CLT result, arbitrary reducible-chain inference or a large-scale RL claim, and reward-sum variance is not policy-gradient estimator variance: exact arithmetic checks the supplied model's calculations, not its realism.
  - Case study: [ergodlab.md](ergodlab.md)
  - Repo: [midya8/ergodlab](https://github.com/midya8/ergodlab)

- **ScoreLab** - Exact policy-gradient distributions, not noisy training curves, built from scratch in pure Python — it makes the variance cost of a familiar baseline inspectable, on supplied finite models where every trajectory, mean and covariance can be computed exactly. A one-step Bernoulli action with P(action=1)=4/5 and reward equal to the action has expected reward 4/5 and exact gradient 4/25; the exact state-value baseline 4/5 gives per-trajectory gradient variance 36/625 — nine times the 4/625 of no baseline — while the variance-minimizing scalar baseline 1/5 makes both possible actions produce the same estimate 4/25, so its variance is zero: an explicit counterexample, not a claim that value baselines usually hurt. A separate three-step, two-state model with two shared policy parameters has 64 positive-probability trajectories and exact gradient [1238843/7962624, -4477/31104]; trace covariances are 5.4508 for the full-return score function, 2.2574 for reward-to-go with no baseline, 1.5773 with the exact time/state value baseline and 1.4625 with the variance-minimizing time/state baseline. The optimal scalar baseline at each time/state node is E[Z_j · G0]/E[||Z_j||²], using the whole reward-to-go gradient vector, not a local reward-weighted average or the exact state value, and ScoreLab returns both sides of the exact excess-variance identity for every supplied baseline. Matrix score controls go further: a fixed matrix B Z at each time/state lets every output coordinate use every score coordinate, minimizing the whole covariance matrix in positive-semidefinite order within that class. On a one-step three-action policy with probabilities 1/6, 1/3, 1/2, features (0,0), (1,0), (0,1) and rewards 0, 1, 2, the exact gradient is [-1/9, 1/3]: no baseline leaves trace variance 269/324, the exact state-value baseline 29/324, the best scalar baseline (64/51) 53/612, and the best matrix control — the exact matrix [[2/3,-2/3],[-1/2,1]] — gives zero on every action: a supplied finite-model example, not a general zero-variance method. On the separate 64-path sequential model the gain is much smaller, scalar-optimal trace 1.462519 against matrix-optimal 1.460918, and its time/state score ranks are [1,0,1,1,1,1], so pretending every score covariance has an inverse would fail; rational RREF solves the singular normal equations with free coordinates set to zero, not a pseudoinverse, and every covariance gap is returned with its positive-semidefinite node terms. 36 tests cover 256 one-step Bernoulli policies with analytic gradients and variance formulas, 24 shared-parameter sequential settings with 48 coordinate derivatives checked by a separate dual-number Cartesian path oracle plus central finite differences, all covariance entries against an independent enumeration, 15 matrix-control settings with exact cross moments and principal minors, and two reproducible SVGs — one embedding the complete exact demo as compressed metadata — with byte-regeneration checks; strict JSON CLI, 1..3 states/actions/features, horizon 0..6, 64 KiB input cap and a six-module wheel verified outside the checkout, Python 3.10..3.13, standard library only. The variance-reduction reasoning follows Greensmith, Bartlett and Baxter (JMLR 2004, Sections 4–6), but ScoreLab does not implement GPOMDP or reproduce that paper's experiments — supplied finite models only, with no environment fitting, actor-critic training, continuous state/action method, learned baseline or large-scale policy-gradient performance claim.
  - Case study: [scorelab.md](scorelab.md)
  - Repo: [midya8/scorelab](https://github.com/midya8/scorelab)

- **BanditLab** - Exact finite-budget Bayesian bandit planning from scratch in pure Python — it makes the value of learning before committing inspectable, on supplied priors where every posterior, policy value and regret can be computed exactly. Two independent Beta-Bernoulli arms: arm 0 has prior Beta(1,1), mean 1/2; arm 1 has prior Beta(5,4), mean 5/9. Each arm's unknown success rate is drawn once from its prior and then stays fixed; pulling an arm returns a unit Bernoulli reward and updates only that arm's posterior. With one pull, choose arm 1; with two pulls both first arms tie and the adaptive-greedy gain is zero; with three pulls the optimal adaptive policy starts with arm 0 despite its smaller current mean — if that first pull succeeds, choose arm 0 next; if it fails, choose arm 1 next — and the full returned state graph determines the last action too. Over the same three-pull budget the optimal adaptive policy expects 187/108, the fully updating posterior-mean greedy policy 46/27, the best fixed-arm commitment 5/3, and a clairvoyant that knows the latent rates beforehand 2 as an upper bound — the adaptive gain over greedy is exactly 1/36, and greedy is not frozen: it updates its posterior after every outcome and picks a new highest-mean arm at every remaining decision. The six evaluated budgets are 1, 2, 3, 4, 6 and 8 pulls; the first SVG's lines connect those values plus the known zero-budget origin. A forward policy audit propagates exact probability mass separately from backward conditional values and accounts for every lost reward as an occupancy-weighted Bellman gap: over three pulls the optimal policy averages 11/6 arm-0 pulls across 14 of 35 graph states with zero loss, greedy averages 2/3 arm-0 pulls across the same 14 states with loss 1/36, and fixed arm 1 averages 0 across 10 of 35 states with loss 7/108 — greedy's entire 1/36 loss is its first-decision gap, while fixed's contributions are 1/36, 8/297 and 1/99, which sum to 7/108, a finite-horizon performance-difference identity, not a causal experiment. An exact information-advantaged upper bound integrates the product of the arms' Beta CDFs: in the demo E[max(theta_0,theta_1)]=2/3, so at three pulls the optimal Bayes regret is 29/108. Strict inputs cap the problem at 1..3 arms, integer starting shapes 1..16, horizon 0..12 and 64 KiB CLI files, with an exact state preflight — binomial(H+2K,2K) posterior/time states — checked before any generation: two arms at horizon 12 need 1,820 states, three arms at horizon 8 need 3,003, and three arms at horizon 12 need 18,564, above the default 4,096 state limit. 34 tests include every complete depth-three two-arm policy tree for 16 prior pairs (2,048 references checked by an independent Beta-moment oracle), every complete depth-two three-arm tree for 64 prior triples (1,728 references, plus a 462-state three-arm graph audit), all 256 initial Beta shape pairs against an independent binomial/Bernstein CDF expression, and 144 independent named-policy history references across 16 prior pairs and three horizons, with two reproducible SVGs, a strict JSON CLI and an isolated six-module wheel. The Bayesian information-state formulation follows Min, Maglaras and Moallemi's Thompson Sampling with Information Relaxation Penalties (Sections 2 and 3), but BanditLab implements the small full finite-state Bellman problem, not that paper's IRS algorithms, Thompson sampling, Gittins indices or a large-scale bandit method. Prescribed supplied-prior calculations only, not simulated trials, empirical bandit rankings, confidence intervals or real-world decision safety.
  - Case study: [banditlab.md](banditlab.md)
  - Repo: [midya8/banditlab](https://github.com/midya8/banditlab)

- **BeliefLab** - Exact rational finite-horizon POMDP planning for two hidden states, built from scratch in pure Python — it makes the value of paying for information inspectable, on supplied models where every belief, tree and envelope can be computed exactly. A directly specified sensing fixture has two hidden states, three actions and a two-action horizon at discount one and uniform initial belief: exploit action 0 rewards +1 in state 0 and -1 in state 1, exploit action 1 reverses those rewards, both with uninformative observations, while Probe costs 1/10 now and reports the state correctly with probability 9/10. A myopic one-action decision picks an exploit action — immediate reward zero beats probe's -1/10 — but two-action planning probes, then exploits on the observation: each observation has probability 1/2, producing posterior (9/10,1/10) or (1/10,9/10), expected second reward 4/5, total -1/10 + 4/5 = 7/10, against zero for either exploit-first sequence over the same horizon. For any starting belief b the exact two-step value is max(2(1-2b), 7/10, 2(2b-1)); Probe is optimal on [13/40,27/40], including a tie at either boundary, and outside that interval the more likely state is exploited. An optimized open-loop baseline checks every fixed action sequence over the same model and horizon — nine two-step sequences, at most 81 under scope — and still uses the correct transition dynamics and every action's reward: at the uniform belief four exploit-only sequences tie at value zero while the contingent optimum is 7/10, the exact feedback gap peaks at 7/10 at b=1/2 with uniform-belief integral 49/400, and a declared control that changes every observation row to (1/2,1/2) and nothing else has gap exactly zero at every belief. A forward execution audit traces every observation history at every time, including impossible histories without fabricated posteriors: state mass is the unconditional joint probability of history and current state, its sum is the reach probability, and summing discounted reward mass over all decisions gives the policy value — the probe policy's uniform-prior accounting is -1/10 + (1/2)(4/5) + (1/2)(4/5) = 7/10, each branch contributing 2/5, not 4/5, and a separate backward tree evaluation must agree exactly with the forward sum. Fixed-tree regret certificates compare a supplied tree against the optimized envelope over every exact interval: the probe tree is optimal on [13/40,27/40] but loses 13/10 at either certain-state prior, and an exploit-0-twice tree has uniform-prior value zero, regret 7/10 and worst regret 4 at belief0=0 while being optimal on [27/40,1] — being optimal at the uniform prior does not make one fixed tree optimal at all priors. Discounted all-belief Bellman-residual brackets connect finite envelopes to infinite optimal value without pretending the finite tree runs forever: a prescribed constant reward 1/100 at discount 99/100 has horizon-zero residual 1/100 but infinite value 1, so the raw residual is not a value-error certificate, and a negative-reward control gives residual -1/100 and exact infinite value -1; a separate perfect-probe fixture (probe perfectly revealing at cost 1/10, discount 1/2) has analytic infinite value max(2|2b-1|, 9/10), and at uniform belief its horizon-three value is 13/20 with global residual range [31/600,1/8], signed shifts [31/300,1/4] and certified infinite-value interval [113/150,9/10] against true value 9/10. 86 tests cover 81 model settings against 2,187 complete depth-two policy trees, a separate 2,187-tree depth-three check, 729 fixed sequences, exact crossing cells, full joint path occupancies and independent belief recursion, with four reproducible SVGs, strict JSON CLI, preflight caps and an isolated ten-module wheel. Supplied-model calculations only, not learned information value, empirical calibration, causal sensor performance or real-world safety.
  - Case study: [belieflab.md](belieflab.md)
  - Repo: [midya8/belieflab](https://github.com/midya8/belieflab)

- **BellmanLab** - Inspect exact values in small supplied discounted models. A residual control separates a small Bellman residual from a small value error. Separate rectangular-scenario and nested-policy workflows expose scenario-row choices, evaluated policies and cap failures. One- and two-budget browser inspectors show occupancy flow, visit-reweighted randomized policies and independently checked primal-dual or infeasibility certificates. Python uses exact fractions; the browser uses BigInt rationals. These are supplied finite dynamics and expected costs, not learned models, per-trajectory safety guarantees or a scalable optimizer. The case keeps the controls, source credits, historical test counts and current source/live-check limits.
  - Case study: [bellmanlab.md](bellmanlab.md)
  - Repo: [midya8/bellmanlab](https://github.com/midya8/bellmanlab)

- **VariLab** - Inference for supplied Ising models, with a full finite-state reference, mean-field coordinate histories, rational Hessian sign certificates, evidence-aware forest sum-product and compatible joint MAP/count/traceback. The two-spin counterexample has a higher ELBO and lower reverse KL but worse marginal means; a zero-residual coordinate fixed point is not an ELBO maximum. Forest methods allow 128 spins. Bounded cyclic sum-product and rational joint MAP allow 64 spins and 8 query indices, with symbolic work preflight before numeric tables. Independent marginal modes or tied local choices need not form a joint optimum. Current checks: 183 tests, six reproducible SVGs and an installed wheel. Enumeration, mean field and curvature are capped at 12 spins; floating enumeration is not rational probability arithmetic. Rational scores and curvature concern represented binary-float inputs. Resource counts are not time or RAM, refused orders are not impossible models, and no learning, causal, calibrated-uncertainty or optimizer-convergence claim follows.
  - Case study: [varilab.md](varilab.md)
  - Repo: [midya8/varilab](https://github.com/midya8/varilab)

- **RobustLab** - Exact linear threat-set audits built from scratch in pure Python — it makes the gap between clean accuracy and certified robustness measurable instead of asserted, for the small class where the inner attack problem can actually be solved: binary affine scores under L1, L2 and L-infinity balls. For signed margin y*(w.x+b), the worst margin in an Lp ball is the clean margin minus epsilon times the dual weight norm, and the implementation returns the actual perturbation attaining that margin — a signed coordinate vertex for L1, an opposite weight direction for L2, a signed corner for L-infinity — plus the attacked coordinates, attained signed margin and zero-margin radius. Both training arms start from zero on the same 30 fixed synthetic rows for 4,000 updates at rate 0.1 with L2 coefficient 0.001, and the only changed training setting is the L-infinity radius, 0 versus 0.30, with no test-driven stopping, seed search or checkpoint selection: ordinary logistic reaches 100% clean accuracy on the shifted holdout but 20% strict robust accuracy at radius 0.30 and fails the >=75% robust gate, while robust logistic accepts 80% clean for 80% robust and passes — a constructed descriptive comparison on a supplied factorial grid, not an image benchmark or a universal claim about the cost of robustness. The robust objective puts the worst margin inside softplus with a separate explicit quadratic weight regularizer; hand-derived selected subgradients are checked against central finite differences away from kinks and against convex subgradient inequalities at zero/tied weights, and the optimizer is fixed-step full-batch descent, not an asserted exact minimizer. A separate exact-audit API makes the float limitation actionable for L1 and L-infinity balls: it interprets each validated input as its actual stored binary float, returns scores, dual norms, radii, worst/attained margins, perturbations and attacked coordinates as reduced numerator/denominator strings, and sets certificate flags from integer signs rather than rounded decimal views — in a selected boundary fixture the float worst margin is approximately +2.081668171e-17 while the rational worst margin is approximately -2.775557562e-18, so the float strict flag is true while the exact flag is false, with a reverse-disagreement fixture and a dyadic exact-zero control that fails both strict flags; these are selected edge cases from a documented finite decimal grid search, not prevalence measurements, and L2 is rejected explicitly because general directions and margins can be irrational. The box-audit API solves L1/L-infinity balls intersected with supplied per-coordinate bounds and returns exact rational primal movement plus a matching dual gain bound — L1 is bounded fractional knapsack, L-infinity separates by coordinate — and a direct dyadic fixture makes the failure mode concrete: weights (4,1), bias -1/4, point (1/16,3/4), positive label and unit box, at L1 radius 5/8 clipping the old all-real attack leaves margin +1/2 while the actual minimum is -1/16, attained by movements (-1/16,-9/16), with exact primal and dual gains both equal to 13/16 and zero gap, so the positive clipped candidate cannot prove robustness; under L-infinity, independent adverse-direction clipping is optimal. 80 unit tests, including independent rational corner enumeration over 240 small model/label configurations, an L2 3-4-5 identity and 10,000-direction circle check, exact first optimizer update and final objective, a separate full orthant-polytope vertex oracle on 468 box configurations, strict CLI errors and resource preflight at a 4,096 row-coordinate-product cap, three reproducible audit SVGs with all plotted values retained in numeric metadata, and CI on Python 3.10..3.13, standard library only. The original training and all-real audit threat domain has no clipping, boxes or preprocessing; the bias is fixed; strict robustness requires worst signed margin >0 and a boundary tie fails conservatively; the identity is exact in real arithmetic but evaluated in ordinary floats, not interval or outward-rounded arithmetic — no nonlinear or multiclass certificate, adaptive attack suite, distributional generalization, calibrated uncertainty or production security guarantee is claimed.
  - Case study: [robustlab.md](robustlab.md)
  - Repo: [midya8/robustlab](https://github.com/midya8/robustlab)

- **CausalLab** - Exact finite structural causal models built from scratch in pure Python — it keeps observation, intervention and counterfactual queries separate, so a strong association cannot be mistaken for what replacing a mechanism would do. A supplied 100-world confounding fixture reverses a +.26 observed association into a -.10 supplied-model intervention difference, and backdoor adjustment using the supplied Z distribution matches intervention enumeration — a synthetic counterexample, not an empirical treatment effect or policy recommendation. Two supplied mechanisms can share the identical observed joint yet disagree on actions and counterfactuals: with X=U, Y=X versus Y=U, P(Y=1 given do(X=1)) is 1 versus .5, and given factual X=0,Y=0 the probability Y would be 1 under X=1 is 1 versus 0. Named multi-world counterfactual joints expose shared-noise response dependence that identical action marginals hide: stable (Y=U) and flip (Y=X xor U) models both have a uniform observed joint and P(Y=1 | do(X=x))=.5 under either action, yet their (Y0,Y1) joints are (.50, 0, 0, .50) versus (0, .50, .50, 0) while the naive product puts .25 everywhere — factual X=0,Y=0 gives (0,0) for stable and (0,1) for flip. Sharp binary response bounds return exact rational attaining joint witnesses from point action marginals instead of choosing a coupling: with .50/.50 marginals the helped and harmed fractions each range [0,.50] with mean effect .00, and with .25/.75 the helped fraction ranges [.50,.75], harmed [0,.25], mean effect +.50; an independent rational reference enumerates all 165 denominator-eight response joints across 81 marginal pairs and checks exact extrema with and without monotonicity, and optional explicit monotonicity rules out flip while zero-probability conditioning rates stay explicitly undefined. The graph-only d-separation API uses ancestor restriction, moralization and connectivity with certificates — ancestor/moral-edge certificates, collider-descendant checks and explicit incoming-edge surgery — and agrees with an independent reference on 49,152 singleton/conditioning/surgery combinations and 7,040 disjoint set queries across all 64 four-node DAGs consistent with one fixed order. A bounded backdoor-set search checks every subset of 0..10 explicitly available candidates and exposes collider bias: in the fixed A→X, A→C←B, B→Y, X→Y graph the empty set passes while adding C alone opens a collider and fails, so search does not prune by assuming valid supersets; an independent reference agrees on 3,072 adjustment sets across 768 searches, and a positive-support adjustment calculation of .60/.50 matches direct interventions. The frontdoor stage checks the established sufficient frontdoor criterion with all three conditions visible even when several fail — directed bypass witnesses and two full ancestral-moral certificates — and an exact-rational functional from a supplied positive joint; the credited latent-confounding motif and a fixed synthetic XOR fixture separate observed conditionals 41/112 and 37/80 from the frontdoor functional 7/16 and 9/16. 164 tests, independent rational references including all 64 tiny Boolean mechanism pairs, six reproducible audit SVGs and CI on Python 3.10..3.13, standard library only. Supplied mechanisms are assumptions, not causes learned from observational data — exhaustive finite enumeration in floating point, with no graph discovery, automatic do-calculus identification, continuous or cyclic models, or empirical confidence intervals.
  - Case study: [causallab.md](causallab.md)
  - Repo: [midya8/causallab](https://github.com/midya8/causallab)

- **ParticleLab** - A scalar bootstrap particle filter built from scratch in pure Python — it audits what a tidy weight summary hides: equal weights and full effective sample size can still mean every particle descends from one badly placed initial draw. Log-domain likelihood weighting, adaptive systematic resampling, filtered versus carried summaries, and full particles/weights/log weights/parent mappings/root-label histories, against an exact scalar linear-Gaussian Kalman reference with an explicit no-transition-at-t0 convention. On a fixed twelve-point linear example with 512 particles over all sixteen seeds 0..15, the mean of per-seed mean RMSEs versus the exact Kalman filter is .0161016904 and the variance RMSE average is .00794734279, with seed 0 plotted without selecting for fit — model-conditional approximation errors, not real-world accuracy or calibration. The support-collapse fixture (twenty zeros, a=1, process std 0, observation std .1, initial mean 0/std 3, 32 particles, seed 0) makes the failure concrete: with resampling threshold fraction 1 the run ends at weight ESS 32 with 1 initial label carried and latent variance 0, while resampling disabled ends at ESS 1.00002950 with 32 labels carried and latent variance .00000308085, against an exact reference mean 0 and latent variance .000499972224 — full ESS after resampling does not recover missing support, and with zero process noise neither method can create a missing state value. A restricted static square-observation reference has exact mean 0 and sign probability 1/2 with modes near +/-2, yet a selected eight-particle run loses one sign while carried ESS is 8/8. A separate terminal-weighted genealogy smoother traces retained parent maps without drawing new paths: on a fixed 64-step dynamic fixture ending with 32 distinct current states but only one retained initial path, time-0 particle variance is 0 while exact smoothed variance is .009521314, and across all sixteen inherited linear seeds the smoothing mean/variance RMSEs versus the exact scalar RTS reference are .0299202464/.0137440904. Optional backward simulation connects retained filtering states outside the parent maps: 512 draws reach two initial indices instead of one genealogical path, with time-0 variance .022290238 still over twice the exact .009521314, and process std below 1e-4 is rejected rather than fabricating a deterministic-transition density. An optional linear observation-conditioned Gaussian proposal exposes per-particle proposal moments and predictive importance weights: across all sixteen fixed seeds at N=32, mean RMSE is similar (.082871/.081548 bootstrap/conditional) but conditional variance RMSE is worse (.037895 versus .035944) — a more informative observation fixture has smaller conditional errors, not a general or causal ranking. 114 unit tests, strict JSON CLI, a reproducible four-panel SVG with all plotted values retained in numeric metadata, and CI on Python 3.10..3.13, standard library only. A scalar supplied-parameter model with finite particles and floating point — no convergence certificate, high-dimensional proposal, learned parameters, robustness, anomaly p-values or coverage guarantee, and evidence estimates are random with logs that are not unbiased log-evidence estimates.
  - Case study: [particlelab.md](particlelab.md)
  - Repo: [midya8/particlelab](https://github.com/midya8/particlelab)

- **SegmentLab** - Exact offline Bayesian Gaussian changepoint inference built from scratch in pure Python — it retains the full finite-partition evidence and marginals, not only one best cut list, so a confident segmentation can be audited instead of trusted. The distinctions it makes inspectable: a single outlier can look like a short, high-confidence segment when the assumed noise is too small; thresholded marginal peaks hide whether cuts occur together; and the most probable whole partition need not minimize expected boundary mistakes. Conjugate Gaussian interval evidence uses centered rolling mean/M2 statistics with no repeated interval rescans; count-capped forward/backward log sums, explicitly normalized admissible Bernoulli-gap priors, all interval/boundary/count marginals and a complete MAP backtrace; pointwise latent-mean uncertainty includes within-segment and between-partition components and excludes future observation noise; seeded complete-partition backward draws plus conditional Gaussian segment means come with finite-RNG limits stated; exact requested-pair cut-inclusion queries allow other cuts, return finite log probabilities, and flag impossible events separately from undefined conditionals; full discrete partition entropy, conditioned-prior KL and dependence of the entire cut vector replace an independent-cut entropy shortcut; constrained minimum expected false/missed boundary-loss decisions keep MAP and infeasible-threshold comparisons separate. On a selected seven-point decision fixture the no-cut decision has lower expected boundary risk, .680000591, than the MAP partition [3] at .869071786 — MAP maximizes posterior probability while `decide` minimizes expected loss under supplied relative costs, and neither number is observed accuracy. The same isolated outlier under two supplied noise scales gives MAP [12,13] at probability .960218 for sigma .15 and MAP [] at .648223 for sigma 2 — model confidence can isolate contamination, not a noise-model quality ranking. On six zeros with a count cap of 2, cuts 2 and 4 each carry marginal mass .011658220257 while their exact joint mass is 0, so multiplying marginals invents a structurally forbidden event; conditioning on an impossible event returns null, not 0. A four-point fixture has posterior entropy 1.029013408 above the prior .836308115 with KL .166962288, so KL is not prior entropy minus posterior entropy. In 10,000 joint draws at seed 19, the four-segment partition K4 has exact mass .000115427 and zero sampled visits — zero visits do not imply zero posterior mass. Same-design replicate mixtures integrate partition and segment-mean uncertainty, then add fresh noise: in a selected five-point outlier fixture a moment-matched Gaussian overstates density at 2 by 1.54 nats, a shape comparison using all fitted observations, not forecasting, leave-one-out evaluation, anomaly p-values or calibrated coverage. Inference is offline — future observations inform earlier cuts — full marginals cost O(N²·K²) work, bounded to 120 observations and 12 requested segments; results are finite sums in floating point, not symbolic arithmetic or formal numerical certificates, and tiny masses can underflow. 104 unit tests, independent exhaustive tiny references, strict JSON infer/sample/joint/information/decide/predictive commands and two regenerable SVGs with all plotted values retained and CI byte-checks. Development fixtures only — no online alert, forecast, credible-band coverage, robust-outlier model, event attribution, causality or model-misspecification guarantee, and relative decision costs are supplied loss units, not money.
  - Case study: [segmentlab.md](segmentlab.md)
  - Repo: [midya8/segmentlab](https://github.com/midya8/segmentlab)

- **HamiltonLab** - Hamiltonian Monte Carlo built from scratch in pure Python — an independent implementation of Neal's Hamiltonian dynamics, no new method: leapfrog integration with explicit signed energy errors, a reversibility check via the unflipped momentum, log-domain Metropolis decisions and a complete transition history in which rejected transitions repeat the old state, because dropping the repeats would bias the output. Demo on disclosed analytic targets, four chains per arm, seeds 71–74, 1,000 retained plus 200 discarded transitions each: on a correlated Gaussian with covariance [[1,.95],[.95,1]], HMC at epsilon .15 and L 10 reaches retained acceptance .979–.987 with pooled means .008864/.018598, variances 1.029658/1.016501 and covariance .973860 against analytic 0/0, 1/1 and .95, while a random walk at proposal SD .15 accepts .765–.800 with means .172653/.195747, variances 1.471826/1.499563 and covariance 1.436712 — same transition counts, different work (52,804 potential-plus-gradient callbacks against 4,804), so the comparison is descriptive, not a sampler ranking. The separated mixture — means (−6,0)/(+6,0), identity component covariance, analytic covariance diag(37,1) — is the point of the tool: HMC accepts .997–1.000 yet each chain makes zero x-sign/mode switches, classical unsplit x R-hat is 6.944902, and pooled summaries that look healthy (positive-x share exactly .5, mean x ≈ −.001131, variance x ≈ 36.420599) conceal disconnected chains, because pooled occupancy was induced by the starts, not learned by mixing. `rank_split_rhat` adds split rank-normalized and folded checks: correlated-Gaussian HMC 1.007774/1.001669, random walk 1.742260/1.331213, separated mixture 1.733936/1.021593 — the mixture maximum is smaller than its classical 6.944902, a different statistic, not evidence of better mixing. Two deterministic counterexamples show why the classical value alone is not enough: a scale difference where classical 0.994987 and rank split 0.989949 look low but folded rank split is 2.117248, and a half-chain shift where classical 0.994987 misses the within-chain shift that rank split 2.115500 exposes. A second failure needs no separated modes: at epsilon √2 on the standard 2-D Gaussian, four leapfrog steps are an identity map in exact arithmetic, so four-step chains accept 1.000 with largest coordinate span 2.81e-14 and mean squared accepted jumps 1.29e-30–1.80e-30, against one-step acceptance .570–.710, span 6.231696 and jumps 2.231222–4.027284 — linear stability and unit determinant do not imply exploration. State-independent uniform trajectory lengths 1–4 restore motion on that resonance (acceptance .845–.910, spans 4.601358–6.454790) but still make zero mode switches in the mixture with lengths 1–10, so randomization is not a solution to separated modes. Analytic harmonic-flow references at exact durations show the same lesson from the other side: at T=2π the expected squared jump is 5.999039e-32, and at T=π it is 4 with conditional variance 1.499760e-32 — position only flips sign — so large squared jumps, zero integration error and invariance do not prove exploration. Finite-window observable audits make the observable-dependence explicit: at the sign flip with N=20 the variance of the mean position is 0 while the variance of the mean q² is 2 (20× the IID value), and random-duration mixtures can give zero position correlation with q²-mean variance still 20× IID — zero correlation of one observable is not independence. A deterministic renderer reproduces the observable-window figure (five panels, shared linear axes, N 1–40) from the analytic references, and CI regenerates and byte-checks the SVG. 82 unit tests cover analytic gradients, leapfrog reversibility, energy error, resonance, rank diagnostics, harmonic references, finite-window and mixture audits and CLI. Offline sample/demo/quadratic/diagnose/harmonic/window/window-mixture CLI and reproducible SVGs. Development fixture only — no convergence certificate, ESS/MCSE estimate, mixing-time guarantee or general sampler ranking claim.
  - Case study: [hamiltonlab.md](hamiltonlab.md)
  - Repo: [midya8/hamiltonlab](https://github.com/midya8/hamiltonlab)

- **TransportLab** - Small entropic transport problems with full plans, explicit marginal errors and separate rounded primal/dual audits. The editable browser differentiates raw-weight normalization and every fixed Sinkhorn update, retaining all plans, tangents, potentials and history rows. One early-weight control gives full tangent 0.010665526 versus requested-marginal candidate -0.019162331; actual marginal-tangent residual terms explain the difference. Twelve controls include null normalized directions, rectangular inputs, zero support and underflow. 62 Python and six Node/actual-Chrome tests, independent Decimal80 differences, native/browser full reports and installed replay. The earlier 46 tests and outputs remain. No adaptive-stop or rounding derivative, changing-support sensitivity, implicit optimum or certified-bound claim.
  - Case study: [transportlab.md](transportlab.md)
  - Repo: [midya8/transportlab](https://github.com/midya8/transportlab)

- **FlowLab** - Affine-coupling normalizing flow from scratch in pure Python — exact 2-D model log density, explicit inverse sampling, bounded log scales and handwritten likelihood gradients, not an exact fit: four alternating affine-coupling layers map data x to standard-normal latent z with a shared 1 -> tanh(12) -> 2 conditioner, scale bounded as tanh(raw_scale) while translations and data are not clipped, and a 200-parameter demo trained by manual backpropagation and minibatch Adam. Demo on a disclosed banana-shaped fixture, x=u, y=0.5*v+0.6*(u*u-1) with independent u,v ~ N(0,1) — 1,024 training points (seed 53), 512 fresh test points (seed 59), flow seed 11, 2,000 updates, batch 32, learning rate 0.003, final checkpoint with no validation or test-based selection and no architecture, schedule or seed sweep: the trained flow gives test NLL 2.188408 against 2.907770 for a train-fitted full-covariance Gaussian and 2.896854 for the same flow untrained, with the known generating density 2.151426 as a report-only reference — descriptive fixture comparisons, not a causal decomposition or a general model ranking. Test roundtrip/logdet residual max 1.78e-15 is an implementation audit, not a global error bound, and sample residuals and outliers remain visible. Conditional slices evaluate the joint log density along one free coordinate with log-max subtraction against underflow, and the trapezoid rule normalizes a piecewise-linear density on the supplied finite interval — moments integrate the interpolation analytically, CDF integrates each segment, quantiles invert by bounded bisection, and the JSON retains every grid point, the original joint log density, normalized density, CDF, requested quantiles, moments and actual model-call count, with both fixed-axis choices working and no unreported adaptive search. The window audit reuses one outer grid and a contiguous, index-aligned subgrid for the inner window with no second round of model evaluations: outer [-4,4] at 513 points, inner indices 192..320 giving [-1,1] at 129 points, 1,539 model evaluations total, and narrowing the window removes 24.35%–31.94% of interpolated mass within the finite outer window (0.243537 at fixed x=0, 0.297166 at x=-1.5, 0.319448 at x=1.5) with inner-minus-outer median shifts +0.161078, -0.191406 and -0.226502 — the removed fractions are not whole-line tail probabilities, they exclude everything outside [-4,4] and may miss structure between grid points, and the inner curve is a new renormalized distribution, not a crop of the outer one. A non-neural adversarial integrand, .0001 + .5*NormalPDF(y; mean=.015625, sd=.0001) on [0,1], shows why grid agreement cannot certify conditional accuracy: both 17- and 33-point grids miss the same narrow peak and report integral 0.0001 against the analytic normal-CDF reference 0.5001, with interpolated means 0.5 despite the actual mean being near 0.01572 — a demonstration about grids, not evidence that the trained model has the same missed peak. 45 tests cover every parameter of tiny flows by central finite differences, an independent finite-difference full Jacobian determinant, both inverse directions, constant-affine density, logdet sign, standard-normal identity, midpoint density integration, strict serialization, bounds, no mutation and CLI, plus aligned window reuse/counts, constant-density offset invariance, exact full-window identity, nested mass monotonicity, the normal erf reference and the missed-peak counterexample. Strict artifacts, offline train/score/gradient/sample/conditional/windows CLI and reproducible SVGs. Development fixture only — no exact-fit, image, anomaly-detection, calibrated-coverage, full-line-normalization or general ranking claim.
  - Case study: [flowlab.md](flowlab.md)
  - Repo: [midya8/flowlab](https://github.com/midya8/flowlab)

- **GraphLab** - Two-layer graph convolutional network built from scratch in pure Python — an independent educational implementation of Kipf and Welling (2017), standard GCN theory, no new method: symmetric normalized propagation S = D^(-1/2)(A + I)D^(-1/2) with H = ReLU(S X W0) and P = softmax(S H W1), applied through sparse neighbor lists — not row normalization — with self loops keeping isolated nodes' own features, and a feature-only MLP control using identity propagation with the same parameterization, initialization, parameter count and update budget. Cross-entropy gradients use training nodes only; validation labels select a checkpoint, including initialization, but never enter the training gradient, and test labels are passed as None during fitting — test features and edges stay visible, so this is transductive node evaluation on one graph, not unseen-graph generalization. Demo on a seed-41 fixture (96 nodes in two equal class communities, 4 noisy features, 483 edges; class means ±0.25, noise SD 1; edges sampled with probability 0.2 within a class and 0.015 across; 8 training + 8 validation + 32 test nodes per class; graph construction deliberately carries class signal): each arm has 72 weights, shared seed 13, Adam learning rate 0.03 and 300 updates, with validation cross-entropy selecting each arm independently among initialization and every update, earliest ties retained — the selected GCN reaches test accuracy 0.96875 (test CE 0.060893) against 0.34375 for the feature-only MLP (selected update 1, CE 0.848734) and 0.515625 for a scrambled-graph GCN (seed 47 relabel, selected update 0, CE 0.693328), with a train-only nearest-class-centroid control at 0.484375 and balanced constant predictions at 0.5 — descriptive fixture results, not causal contributions or a general GCN ranking. A six-cycle and two disconnected triangles with identical constant features and degree 2 give identical node probabilities, an exact counterexample for this architecture, not a claim about all graph networks. `margin_attribution` / `explain` differentiates a target node's chosen class-logit margin with respect to all supplied input coordinates, returning full signed gradient and gradient-times-input matrices, per-node sums, the margin, the sum residual and the two-hop receptive field: on a hand-set path fixture the node contributions are [0.181666667, 0.341907943, 0.074981603, 0, 0, 0], summing to margin 0.5985562126 with residual 0.0, and node 2's first coordinate contributes −0.0136082763, so unsigned magnitudes alone would change the answer — for this bias-free, positively homogeneous ReLU model and the all-zero reference the signed x·gradient sum equals the margin, but a one-node ReLU(x) counterexample at x=−1 has zero gradient and zero x·gradient while replacing x with +1 moves the margin from 0 to 1, so local derivatives do not measure arbitrary finite interventions. `edge_sensitivity` / `edges` is a different question: it reruns the fixed model after deleting each single original edge separately and recomputing all degrees and normalization, returning every signed margin change — node 3's input-feature derivative is zero, yet deleting edge 2-3 changes the target margin by +0.016852 because node 2's degree changes, while deletions 3-4 and 4-5 have no effect in this fixture. A preflight budget requires 1 + number of edges model forwards before any model call (default cap 256, supported 1–8193; the five-edge demo takes exactly six), and an over-budget request fails rather than silently sampling. Small undirected unweighted graphs only (1–128 nodes, 1–16 features, hidden width 1–32, 2–8 classes), no edge features, attention, pooling, minibatching, dropout, weight decay, gradient clipping, calibration or confidence guarantee; the paper's full training recipe and citation-network benchmarks are not reproduced. 42 unit tests cover every parameter of tiny reference models by finite differences in both modes, sparse-vs-dense forward arithmetic, independent degree normalization, permutation-equivariant predictions and training, mask isolation, stable unclipped cross-entropy, selection vs independent prefix refits, artifacts and CLI. Strict artifacts, offline train/predict/explain/edges CLI and reproducible SVGs. Development fixture only — no causal, probability-attribution, unseen-graph or general ranking claim. Joint deletion is now a complete census rather than an additive guess: 51 Python tests retain all 42 originals, with 180 seeded dense/bitmask cases, 70-digit Decimal checks of all 16 counterexample sets, Python/JavaScript agreement on 46 reports and 559 subsets, and NumPy checking all 559 logit vectors (max difference 8.88e-16). In the hand-set counterexample (features [-4,-4,6,6], five edges, baseline margin 0.5534180126) all five single deletions preserve class 1, yet the pair e1=(0,2), e3=(1,2) gives margin -0.6666666667 and class 0 against a singleton extrapolation of +0.3151513784 (residual -0.9818180451); five of the 16 empty/single/pair sets change the class, and the minimum-margin pair e1/e2 reaches -1.0606601718. Preflight sums binomial coefficients through min(k,m) (native limits k=0..8, 1..65,536 forwards); the 483-edge graph at k=2 exceeds the cap while k=1 fits. Five Node/Chrome tests cover 24 responsive views, 568 subset selections and six natively replayed trusted downloads. Bounded binary64 fixed-model sensitivity census over original edges only — not a causal effect or a new algorithm. [Source](https://github.com/midya8/graphlab). [Design contracts](https://github.com/midya8/graphlab/blob/main/DESIGN.md).
  - Case study: [graphlab.md](graphlab.md)

- **DiffuseLab** - Learned 2-D diffusion from scratch in pure Python — standard DDPM theory, no new method: a linear beta schedule, closed-form forward noising and a 1-based timestep contract, with a `7 -> tanh(48) -> 2` noise predictor (two coordinates, normalized time and four sine/cosine features; 482 learned parameters, exact MSE gradients, minibatch Adam) and epsilon-parameterized DDPM reverse means using the forward posterior variance as fixed reverse variance — no final-step noise, clipping, EMA, learned variance or checkpoint selection. Demo on a development fixture of eight equally likely centers on a radius-2 circle with isotropic noise SD 0.12 (1,024 training points, seed 31; 256 fresh reference points, seed 37; network seed 7, training draws seed 8, sample seed 17; no seed, architecture, schedule or parameter search): with 256 samples the learned DDPM reaches all 8/8 modes with mean nearest-center distance 0.378199, 60.55% within 0.4 and symmetric nearest-neighbor squared distance 0.037103, against 20.100807/0.00%/0/8/259.754704 for the same network untrained and 0.977930/7.42%/1/8/0.391052 for a standard Gaussian prior — the untrained chain is an implementation control, not a competitive baseline. Fresh-draw noise-prediction MSE (seed 43) is 0.356809 trained vs 0.962031 untrained. The learned model is imperfect: 39.45% of samples sit farther than 0.4 from any center and nearest-center bins are [16, 36, 23, 28, 23, 37, 43, 50], not balanced; a deliberately collapsed generator passes center-distance metrics but fails coverage and the symmetric metric in a test. The same trained model samples by DDIM on a selected timestep grid with eta in [0,1] — no retraining or checkpoint change: 8 steps saves 87.5% of predictor calls (61.72% within 0.4, 8/8 modes) and 16 steps saves 75% (61.72%), against DDPM's 64 calls at 60.55% — but the larger near-center fraction does not establish better quality, since every DDIM arm has worse symmetric reference distance (0.210661 at 8 steps, 0.188649 at 16, 0.175727 at 64, vs DDPM 0.037103); full-grid eta=1 matches DDPM arithmetic with matched draws, and call savings count actual predictor invocations, not measured runtime, FLOPs or memory. Strict diffuselab_v1 JSON artifacts, seeded training and sampling, offline train/sample/DDIM CLI, 33 unit tests and Python 3.10–3.13 CI. Development fixture only — no walltime speedup, image-generation or general ranking claim, and the fixture was visible during development, not an untouched benchmark.
  - Case study: [diffuselab.md](diffuselab.md)
  - Repo: [midya8/diffuselab](https://github.com/midya8/diffuselab)
- **ChainLab** - Separate prefix evidence, supported path decisions and whole-path uncertainty in hidden Markov models. The rational workbench shows MAP, supported expected matches and supported product marginals choosing different paths, while unconstrained marginal modes can be impossible. Locked and independent chains share the same state marginals but different path entropy. The prefix inspector separates filtering and prefix-only forecasts from hindsight; the original unsmoothed Baum-Welch trainer retains its fixed synthetic controls and scores. Current checks: 58 Python tests, 10 Node tests with actual Chrome, independent tiny-path references, a standalone rational-report verifier and isolated installed replay. Probabilities and path objectives are exact in the rational workbench; entropy is approximate and model-conditional, not calibrated uncertainty, true-state recovery or a quality ranking. [Equations and limits](https://github.com/midya8/chainlab/blob/main/PATHS.md).
  - Case study: [chainlab.md](chainlab.md)
  - Repo: [midya8/chainlab](https://github.com/midya8/chainlab)
- **KernelLab** - Exact Gaussian-process regression and finite-grid expected improvement built from scratch in pure Python — standard GP theory, no new method: an isotropic squared-exponential covariance with a fixed supplied constant mean and iid Gaussian observation noise, five explicit parameters (length_scale, signal_variance, noise_variance, jitter, prior_mean — signal and noise are variances, not standard deviations), conditioned by Cholesky with triangular solves; production code never inverts a dense matrix, and a Gauss-Jordan inverse exists only in tests as an independent tiny reference. The training covariance is K+(noise+jitter)I; nonpositive pivots fail — no adaptive jitter, PSD projection, parameter normalization or silent retry — and tiny negative posterior diagonals within max(1e-12, signal_variance*1e-10) are clamped and counted while material negatives fail. An analytic one-point posterior checks mean, latent variance, LOO and log marginal likelihood, and LOO is a row-level conditional diagnostic with fixed assumptions: d_i=(A^-1)_ii from the squared norm of L^-1 e_i, mean y_i−alpha_i/d_i, effective observation variance 1/d_i. Demo on a seed-19 fixture (15 training inputs in [−3,3], truth sin(1.5x)+0.15x, Gaussian noise SD .12; 101 noisy test observations): length .8 gives observation RMSE .6242366399, mean NLL .1040604253 and 100/101 pointwise 2-SD coverage, while length 5 gives 1.0038088074, 12.1309060441 and 22/101, against an unconditioned .7879412723/1.2321062355 — the wide-kernel adverse arm is kept visible, not tuned away, and the fixture is constructed to need shorter-scale variation, not an untouched benchmark. Training LML −7.418945/−210.975199 and LOO mean NLL .069448/15.090203 are reported without selection. `suggest` is deterministic maximization only and requires zero observation noise: EI=delta·Phi(delta/sigma)+sigma·phi(delta/sigma), with the exact deterministic limit max(delta,0) at zero variance; exact binary64 matches to training rows are excluded, duplicates are errors, and an all-known pool returns status "exhausted" with a null suggestion. An offline optimization demo on sin(2x)+0.4cos(5x)−0.05x² over an 81-point grid gives, at nine evaluations each, best observed 1.045544 at x=−2.5 and finite-grid simple regret 0.000163 for EI, against 0.972762/0.072944 for farthest-point space filling and 0.646233/0.399473 for one seeded uniform random control (grid oracle 1.045706 at x=−2.4) — one objective, one seed, no parameter sweep and no general speedup claim. Dependency-free Python 3.10+, 43 Python tests and four Node/Chrome tests, fit/predict/response JSON CLI, reproducible SVG and Python 3.10–3.13 CI. Live v0.2 adds a [target-response inspector](https://midya8.github.io/kernellab/) that keeps both original GP models fixed: edit one observed target and inspect all 15 signed response weights — the conditional mean moves while latent/observation variances stay unchanged. Independent browser LDL versus native Cholesky checks cover 202 query witnesses and 3,030 direct edited-target predictions, plus high-precision tiny controls and shared-axis endpoint checks; original misspecification results and optimization traces are unchanged. Signed weights are not causal attribution or calibrated confidence, and refitting invalidates the fixed-kernel contrast. The installed wheel reproduces eight modules and seven commands. [Derivation and numerical limits](https://github.com/midya8/kernellab/blob/main/RESPONSE.md).
  - Case study: [kernellab.md](kernellab.md)
  - Repo: [midya8/kernellab](https://github.com/midya8/kernellab)
- **BoostLab** - Standard gradient boosting built from scratch in pure Python — no new theory: binary Bernoulli classification on finite numeric rows, a logit plus a stable sigmoid, stable softplus loss, per-row gradients y − sigmoid(z) checked by central finite differences, and a training-only intercept from half-count smoothed prevalence. Each round fits a greedy squared-error regression tree to the current residuals with fixed shrinkage; default leaves are mean residuals in [−1,1], and an optional Newton refinement replaces each leaf with sum(y−p)/(sum(p(1−p))+L2) with the unshrunk step capped (L2 in [0,1000000], default 0; cap in (0,10], default 3) — the split objective stays residual-SSE, so this is an educational variant, not a clone of gbm/sklearn/XGBoost. Only training rows and labels determine the intercept, residuals, splits and leaf values; validation logloss picks the earliest prefix improving by over 1e-12, including round zero, and the API never accepts test data — a separate demo reports test metrics only after fitting. Demo on seed-7 256/128/512 rows (class is the sign of the coordinate product, flipped with probability 0.05): stumps keep round zero at .4980 accuracy/.7060 logloss, while their unselected 80-round checkpoint reaches .6367/.7366 at 240 nodes; the depth-2 selected 80-round model has 556 nodes at .8906/.3400; the Newton depth-2 arm (L2=1, cap 3) has validation select round 35 with 237 nodes at .900390625/.3341242425, and its last round 80 sits at .8984375/.3468631810 with 538 nodes — the setting was not chosen from a Newton grid search, the fixture is development data already seen, and the comparison does not isolate a causal curvature or regularization benefit. Dependency-free Python 3.10+, 36 Python tests and five Node/Chrome tests, train/predict/path JSON CLI/API, offline demo and Python 3.10–3.13 CI. Version 0.2 adds a live training-path inspector ([Try the training path](https://midya8.github.io/boostlab/) · [Derivation and numerical limits](https://github.com/midya8/boostlab/blob/main/PATH.md)): all three original arms retain all 80 fitted trees even when validation selects zero, 80 or 35, so the inspected prefix is separated from the original selection and selected/last logits are shown for the same input, with all 243 train/validation/report-only-test metric states on a common zero-based loss axis. Editing either coordinate clears the historical fixture label. Every tree route, included/omitted term and complete model can be inspected and exported, and no fitting, selection, model API or uploads run on the page. The native path and an independent browser implementation agree on 4,860 original sample/prefix states and 1,458 edited states, with 78 full route witnesses; independent Fraction sums and recursive scalar metric calculations check all 243 metric states. Actual Chrome checks every original state, 180 UI configurations, physical keyboard input, invalid/stale witness refusal, clipboard failures/success and JSON Blobs; 320/390/1280 layouts and expanded mobile routes/JSON are tested and visually inspected. The installed wheel imports all seven modules, executes all five main commands and reproduces all three original fits, histories and witnesses outside checkout. The original 29 tests remain within the 36 Python tests, and the five Node/Chrome tests add independent inference and UI coverage. The unchanged fitting implementation updates logits incrementally and archived prefix metrics use the existing initial + rate*math.fsum(leaves) inference; browser compensation agrees within checked numerical tolerances, not bitwise or exact-real guarantees; near-zero threshold labels can depend on rounding; algebraic tree terms are neither feature attribution nor causal explanations; sigmoid is not calibrated confidence; same round budget is not equal capacity; Newton also changes regularization/capping; and the development fixture was inspected.
  - Case study: [boostlab.md](boostlab.md)
  - Repo: [midya8/boostlab](https://github.com/midya8/boostlab)
- **ShapleyLab** - Exact small-feature Shapley attribution built from scratch in pure Python: the standard Shapley/SHAP formula over an empirical-background replacement game — no new theory — with every coalition enumerated (2^d values by integer bit mask) and available for audit, a scalar score callback API and a strict JSON CLI. Demo on a product scorer at x=(2,3,99) with prediction 6: a zero background gives [3,3,0] but a shifted background gives [2,3,0] — the reference choice changes the question, not the model prediction; jointly sampled missing features preserve their mutual dependence, and a diagonal-background counterexample has zero attribution and zero efficiency residual while singleton hybrids sit off the observed manifold, so additivity does not certify explanation validity. Calls are budgeted up front ((2^d − 1)·B + 1; 3 features/2 background rows require 15 calls), and an independent factorial-ordering reference checks dimensions 1–6 alongside linearity, symmetry, dummy and feature-permutation tests. Optional atomic groups move correlated blocks together as single players — a three-way product gives grouped [1/2, 1/2], not the post-hoc [2/3, 1/3] from summing individual values — up to 64 raw features and 12 groups, with no within-group credit split reported and no cross-group dependence guarantee. Dependency-free Python 3.10+, 49 Python tests and four Node/actual-Chrome tests, JSON CLI/API, offline demo and Python 3.10–3.13 CI. Optional seeded permutation mode handles up to 64 individual features with cached coalitions and descriptive Monte Carlo standard errors — a two-permutation product gives [0.5, 0.5, 0] despite exact [1/3, 1/3, 1/3], so zero observed Monte Carlo error is not an accuracy bound — with no confidence guarantee and no sampled groups. v0.2 adds a live replacement and permutation ledger: the inspector exposes every background replacement row, coalition value, weighted predecessor term and complete small player ordering; native audits log actual calls to the original exact engine and cached paths add no score evaluations; the independent browser recomputes seven fixed public synthetic games under bounded target edits, not a user callback; atomic grouped credits stay distinct from post-summed individual credits. 49 Python tests plus four Node/actual-Chrome tests check 95 coordinate edits, 128 Fraction games, 64 independent BigInt product games, all 44 original coalitions, 18 players and 30 orderings; the installed 11-module/four-command reproduction retains the original exact/grouped/sampled modes and all prior demo reports; signed zero-centered bars, hybrid provenance, invalid-state clearing and JSON witnesses are checked at 320/390/1280 widths. The original diagonal-reference squared-difference game still assigns zero credits through unrealistic singleton hybrids, and the P=2 sample still assigns zero estimated error to a wrong dummy-looking credit — efficiency alone validates neither; no new model, training, causal attribution, representative-background, confidence or explanation-quality claim. v0.3 adds a two-level Owen mode: the same three-way product can assign [1/3, 1/3, 1/3] to individual features, [1/2, 1/2] to two atomic groups, and [1/4, 1/4, 1/2] to Owen members when a, b must arrive together — one score and background, different allowed orders, so equal splitting and post-summing ordinary credits are not the same rule. The mode keeps every background-replacement evaluation, member coefficient, quotient-group marginal and complete admissible ordering; its native checker imports no generator and independently replays the declared synthetic score and full allocation report, while the independent browser computes permutation-count weights, lets you edit the supplied model and partition, and downloads evidence the native CLI can verify. 72 Python tests include 556 signed game/partition cases checked across 50,362 independent Fraction orderings, 14,668 unanimity-support/partition cases and separate primitive-mutation checks; 132 complete native/JavaScript reports agree, plus 206 broad-magnitude, half-even, subnormal and cancellation reports independently native-verify, and an original BigInt binary64-unit summation helper fixes a pre-release double-rounding failure without widening tolerance. Actual Chrome checks all 12 original controls at 320/390/1280, 12 trusted completed native-verified downloads, and raw/sigmoid six-feature/32-background requests with 2017 evaluations and 720 paths each; fresh installed-wheel checks preserve all 95 prior edit archives and original modes, all 24 feature/PR checks, 12 main checks and three Pages jobs passed, and all 45 deployed browser states match the tested unmasked pixels. Established coalitional-value theory with original supplied synthetic controls; no new allocation theorem or measured explanation-quality result, and standalone supplied tables support up to eight features while full declared-score/browser reports cover up to six features and 32 background rows. [Live editable Owen inspector](https://midya8.github.io/shapleylab/owen.html) · [Method, sources and boundaries](https://github.com/midya8/shapleylab/blob/main/OWEN.md) · [Reviewed change](https://github.com/midya8/shapleylab/pull/2). [Derivation, six sources and limits](https://github.com/midya8/shapleylab/blob/main/LEDGER.md).
  - Case study: [shapleylab.md](shapleylab.md)
  - Repo: [midya8/shapleylab](https://github.com/midya8/shapleylab)
- **PolicyLens** - Offline policy evaluation for logged contextual bandits built from scratch in pure Python: standard IPS, SNIPS, DM and DR estimators — no new statistical method — with strict schema validation, explicit support and overlap diagnostics (ESS, max/mean/zero-weight fraction) and clipping trade-offs labeled, not hidden. Demo on a constructed two-context design with known target value 0.78: twenty deterministic mean-reward rows reproduce IPS/SNIPS/DR = 0.78 against a wrong direct estimate of 0.5, and clipping at 1 moves IPS to 0.185, SNIPS to 0.6166667 and DR to 0.535 while ESS rises 4.9231 to 8.4706; a 2000-row seed-13 Bernoulli log with oracle q gives DR = 0.7532475, not exact population truth — selected development fixtures, not a power/variance study. An optional crossfit command fits held-out categorical reward means (five folds, seed 17, fixed prior): direct 0.7496455 and DR 0.7509088 on the same noisy log, explicitly not closer to truth than oracle DR 0.7532475 and not an estimator ranking; row-iid splits do not protect clustered/time data. Target-positive/behavior-zero actions get a no-nonparametric-identification label instead of a silent zero-weight success. The v0.2 live row-contribution ledger exposes every observed propensity, raw/used weight, direct prediction, signed residual, IPS/DM/DR additive term, normalized SNIPS term, unsupported target action and signed cap removal across five fixed synthetic cases, with an independent browser evaluator checking bounded target-mixture and single-row reward edits against native Python witnesses; the signed shared-axis cap path keeps negative DR visible, delete-one-row reports hold policy and predictions fixed, and zero observed target mass leaves SNIPS undefined rather than substituting a value. 36 Python and four Node/actual-Chrome tests include 160 independent Fraction cases, 80 independent integer-grid browser-oracle cases, 95 native/browser edit witnesses and 589 audited rows plus 589 deletion reports, with installed-wheel checks importing seven modules and running all five commands outside checkout. Original four reports, source data and estimator implementations are unchanged. Cap paths and ESS are descriptive, not confidence, tuning or deployment advice. Dependency-free Python 3.10+, JSON CLI/API, offline demo and Python 3.10-3.13 CI. [Live row ledger](https://midya8.github.io/policylens/). [Equations and limits](https://github.com/midya8/policylens/blob/main/LEDGER.md).
  - Case study: [policylens.md](policylens.md)
  - Repo: [midya8/policylens](https://github.com/midya8/policylens)
- **DriftLens** - Embedding distribution tests from scratch in pure Python: unbiased RBF MMD two-sample tests with a fixed kernel and explicit assumptions, one cached pooled kernel re-indexed for permutations, exact tiny-sample enumeration and seeded Monte Carlo permutations with nonzero finite p-values. Demo on seed-13 30x4 standard-normal samples: same-generator MMD squared -0.004871, p=0.550; adding +2 to each current coordinate gives 0.634539, p=0.002 at the 1/500 resolution floor — selected development fixtures, not a power/type-I error study. A tiny separated 2-vs-2 example has exact p=1/3 over six allocations. Dependency-free Python 3.10+, 45 unit tests, JSON CLI/API, offline demo and Python 3.10-3.13 CI. v0.2 adds a rational supplied-Gram exact-orbit inspector that makes kernel-family selection visible: every allowed label allocation, every diagonal-excluding MMD² and tied upper-tail rank, recalibrated as the smallest rank over the same shared orbit — prespecified blocks keep their observed reference counts and frozen labels stay frozen. In one original six-row, three-view development control at alpha 1/5, the uncorrected smallest raw p rejects 8/20 allowed allocations, the global test 4/20 and Holm-any 0/20 — complete finite-orbit counts, not measured real-world false-positive rates or a power benchmark. All 58 Python tests pass, preserving all 45 prior tests and the original floating RBF modes, plus four Node tests; checks include 50 rational orbits/1,410 allocations, 729 matrices against independent principal-minor PSD acceptance and 753 single-leaf evidence mutations, with 95 complete native/JavaScript reports matching exactly across 3,289 allocations, 12,600 scores and 179,225 global-tail memberships. Actual deployed Chrome covers eleven controls at 320/390/1280, all 372 allocation views and 51 matrix views, eleven trusted completed native-verified downloads and the ten-row/six-kernel/252-allocation boundary. Bounds: tiny exact orbits only, 4–10 pooled rows and 1–6 supplied kernels; established finite randomization/minimum-p ideas, not a new test, automatic bandwidth optimizer or MMDAgg. The suite verifies 95 full native/browser witnesses, 9,367 row/deletion records, 55,328 class Brier terms, 160 independent 70-digit Decimal games and 80 integer/uniform controls, with actual UI checks visiting all 493 positions/50 bins, and an installed wheel rebuilds the original full training/logits/report and all 95 witnesses outside the checkout, importing 7 modules and running 5 commands. Limits stay explicit: standard temperature scaling and proper losses, not a new calibration theorem; ECE/MCE depend on bins, finite counts and only top-class probabilities; lower NLL/Brier does not isolate calibration; fixed-temperature deletion is not a validation refit, causal effect or uncertainty interval; positive scaling preserves real-arithmetic argmax while binary64 rounding/underflow limits are explicit; the original rounded-NLL subtraction gives the .0076 oracle gap while unchanged full precision .007543134492483228 rounds to .0075 in the new ledger; no stronger deployment guarantee. - **DistillLab** - Knowledge distillation built from scratch in pure Python: a teacher trained on abundant data, a 10x smaller student trained on a tenth of the data, and the measured gain compared against matched control arms — pseudo-labels, soft-label distillation, label smoothing, and a permuted-target control at exactly chance. 24 Python tests and four Node/actual-Chrome tests. v0.2 adds a live student-update inspector: select any original trained student arm or its original initialization, one of the 80 student-training rows, temperature, soft weight and step size, and independent Python and browser evaluators expose hard/soft class derivatives, every simultaneous parameter update, hidden activations, stable CE/entropy/KL terms and all original 80 training/320 test predictions, with full input/report JSON; each entered state starts from its chosen frozen model and test labels never enter the gradient. A supplied two-hidden-unit control makes the finite-step limitation visible: selected-row loss rises from 1.3466 to 3.6897 at eta=2 although the local directional derivative is negative. The suite verifies 36 complete native/browser witnesses with 8,900 parameter records and 14,001 evaluation positions, 160 independent 70-digit Decimal logit controls, 80 uniform-class controls, every-parameter finite differences on the original initialization and the overshoot network, and 21 original backprop/update states, and an installed wheel rebuilds the original teacher and six student fits, full data/logits/report and all witnesses outside the checkout, importing seven modules and running all four commands. The new inspector uses stable, unclipped logit-space objectives, while the original helper functions clamp probabilities at 1e-12 and their gradient formula is the ordinary unclipped derivative, the distinction below that floor explicit; T changes target entropy and objective scale, so loss values across different T do not rank generalization; a lower local loss is not a generalization, calibration, causal-mechanism or deployment guarantee, and one step is not a training run. Earlier coordinate inspector retained:  Same model, different factor-SGD step: equivalent factor coordinates (`B' = BC`, `A' = C^-1 A`) preserve `BA` in real arithmetic, but ordinary factor SGD need not preserve the next merged model — the inspector exposes the full finite-step second-order identity `delta(BA) = -eta_A BB^T G - eta_B GA^T A + eta_A eta_B G(BA)^T G`, and scalar compensation `eta_A' = eta/c^2`, `eta_B' = eta*c^2` reproduces the original next merged model in real arithmetic, a coordinate identity rather than a new optimizer. At width 2, epoch 30, first example, learning rate 0.1, scale 4, the ordinary next-matrix difference is 0.0056767304 while scalar compensation gives residual 0 in binary64. All 124 original epoch boundaries (widths 1/2/4/8) remain inspectable with full matrix gauges, 31-scale sweeps, per-example outputs and complete report/input/archive exports, with no best-state selection or retraining from the controls.
